**spatial** if the difference is **≤ 0** in the pooled estimate. Anything else — mixed signs, differences inside
(−0.00, +0.10], seed disagreement — is **unresolved** and is reported as such, with the number of events, the bin
boundaries and the fallback count (events whose bin contained no candidate surrogate).

**Why the data had to be re-logged (16:05).** The old artifacts keep one snapshot per 1000 steps, so a daughter's birth
almost never coincides with a logged snapshot and the structures present at her birth are unknown: the redesign keeps
925 events only in principle. X70 was therefore re-run with `--sample-every 50` and field snapshots every 200 steps,
nothing else changed. Anchor: the seven rows the old artifacts kept (steps 1000…6000), `uid_pairs` and `uid_outcomes`
match the old run bit-for-bit in both arms, so logging did not touch the world; only the record got denser (121 rows
instead of 7). This is a re-logging of an existing experiment, not a new one, and the numbers of X70 itself are
unchanged.

**Third control, same registration.** The best available stand-in for "the place" is not another component but the field
itself, so for every event the local environment at the daughter's birth is measured from the snapshot within 200 steps:
mean `v` and occupied fraction inside a radius-8 disc around her centroid. Three uses: (1) does the environment predict
both ends — if it does not, the confound story is empty; (2) does the parent–daughter correlation survive **inside**
local-environment tertiles; (3) does it survive as a rank partial correlation controlling distance, occupancy and the two
local field statistics. Same band: the effect must stay above +0.10 in the pooled estimate and in ≥4 of 5 seeds to be
called transmission rather than place.

**Что это значит для утреннего вопроса.** Правильная среда даёт **ограниченную** наследственность: одно поколение,
точность около трети, линии дальше без дополнительного канала не возникают. Это та же категория «limited heredity»,
на которую указывали ответы (мультистабильность морфологий), и это результат, а не ноль.

**Result after repair (17:20).** The repair found a second, larger defect before it could answer the first question: in a
neutral world (`beta = 0`, `eps = 0`) the geometry does not depend on the seed, so the five "seeds" were one world with
five inert trait noises — `uid_pairs`, areas and peaks identical, only `s` differing. The 925 events were 185 events copied
five times and the pairing null (p95 = 0.054) was five copies wide. `--init-jitter` (seed-derived offset and ±20 % radius
of the starting blob, inert when off, anchor bit-for-bit) produced five real worlds: 1034 events, per-world correlations
0.491 / 0.468 / 0.566 / 0.423 / 0.548 (mean 0.499), each above its own pairing null (p95 = 0.11–0.13), slopes 0.38–0.57.

All three controls were re-run on those worlds. Nearest-distance surrogate: observed **0.582** against **−0.003**
(difference +0.585, cluster bootstrap +0.47…+0.68, 5/5 worlds above +0.10), and the four distance bins read 0.768 / 0.661 /
0.673 / 0.225 — parent-specific at every distance, weakest far away. Occupancy tertiles 0.422 / 0.581 / 0.433 with no
monotone crowding story. Field tertiles **0.183 / 0.488 / 0.630**: heredity is weak in the driest third of the field and
strong in the richest, while the environment does not predict the daughter's area (−0.022) or the parent's (0.111) — so
richness **modulates** heredity instead of causing it. Partial rank correlation controlling distance, occupancy and both
local field statistics: **0.545**.

**Verdict.** Size heredity in the neutral world is real, specific to the parent–daughter pair, and survives the proximity
control that the four external answers demanded. It is partial: about half the parent's deviation is erased within one
division (slope 0.45). The homogeneous arm remains **unresolved** (73 events, 1 of 3 estimable worlds above its null), and
the earlier 0.294 for it was twenty events copied five times. Report:
`reports/2026-11-01-x70-spatial-control-result.md`; defects: `reports/2026-11-01-x70-two-design-defects.md`; aggregates
`l1_field_x70r_size.json` and `l1_field_x70_spatial_strata.json`.

**Correction, 17:20 (replication).** Everything above was measured in a `beta = 0` world, where the seed does not move the
geometry: the `lifetimes` blocks of the five seeds are byte-identical, so the table's "0/5" column is one world, not five.
The rerun (`scripts/x68r_series.py`, `--init-jitter`, 30 runs) keeps the measurement and gives five real worlds per rule:

| rule | hetero medians (5 worlds) | homog medians (5 worlds) | direction | worlds |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.3 loose | 5999 ×5 | 2846, 3985, 4030, 3985, 3091 | hetero longer | 5/5 |
| 0.5 medium | 4400, 4505, 5999, 3777, 5999 | 2846, 3985, 1565, 3985, 2713 | hetero longer | 4/5 |
| 0.7 strict | 871, 1128, 1417, 1118, 712 | 2846, 3985, 1565, 3985, 2713 | hetero **shorter** | 5/5 |

So "the ordering reverses at all three rules" is **withdrawn**: the reversal is unanimous at the loose rule, four of five at
the medium rule, and at the strict rule the direction goes back — there heterogeneous components live 712–1417 steps
against 1565–3985, which is the published direction. The rule does not merely move the magnitude; it moves the sign, and
the old table's 2607 at the strict cutoff was one draw of the starting blob. Report:
`reports/2026-11-01-x68-identity-replicated.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x68r_identity.json`.

## 10eq. Is the medium multistable? Two morphologies at one inflow would make heredity discrete (X71, 01.11, идёт)

> **02.11, 04:35 — superseded in part.** X84 re-read X71's own saved fields with an angular spectrum and found that the two "modes"
> (radial bins 8 and 11) are the **stripe axis** and the **checkerboard forcing diagonal**, which share a radial bin because the
> forcing line sits at `|k| = q√2`. The morphological period is single (`q'` = 5–9, never 11) and what varies with inflow is the
> symmetry `s` (0.38 → 0.92). Read this section with §10fg: the experiment ran as registered, its spectral reading does not survive,
> and the numbers below are the radial ones it produced.

X70 left the size signal graded: a daughter follows her parent at about half strength, so the memory of form lasts one
generation and cannot accumulate. A **multistable** medium would change that class of answer: if two morphologies are
stable at the same parameters, a daughter can fall into the parent's branch and the lineage carries a discrete state instead
of a blurred number. That is the falsifier the four external answers proposed, and it is the first question in this line
that asks about the *shape of the heredity* rather than its presence.

Design (preregistered in `reports/2026-11-01-x71-multistability-plan.md`, amended once after the pilot and before the
series): sweep the inflow `F ∈ {0.025 … 0.055}` at fixed `k = 0.06`, five worlds per cell in each arm (heterogeneous
`amp 0.5`, homogeneous `amp 0.0`), grid 96, `dt = 1.0`, 6000 steps, seeds made real by `--init-jitter` (a `beta = 0` world
ignores its seed — `docs/design-checks.md`). The readout is the radial structure factor of `v` at step 6000: peak position
`q*` (Fourier bins, wavelength `96/q*` cells), the peak's share, the strongest second mode, and `β₀`/`β₁` at threshold 0.2.

A split is called only if it is wider than the instrument: two clusters of `q*` separated by **≥ 3 bins**, each holding
**≥ 2 worlds**, or a single run keeping two modes with shares both ≥ 0.2. The positive control is that the peak position
responds to the inflow (≥ 3 bins between the extremes); monotonicity is reported but was withdrawn as a gate after the pilot
showed 9, 8, 11, 11, 11 — spot size versus inflow is a different question than the one asked here. The pilot also located
the spots-to-labyrinth boundary between `F = 0.035` and `0.040` (β₀ 31 → 4, β₁ 3 → 12), which is why the sweep has headroom
on both sides.

Status: **closed, positive.** At `F = 0.035` (k = 0.06) five independently started worlds split into two wavelengths —
12.0 cells (q = 8 bins, 2 worlds) and 8.73 cells (q = 11 bins, 3 worlds) — and **all five runs carry both modes at once**,
each above a fifth of the spectral power. Outside the band the medium is single-valued (12.0 cells at F <= 0.030, 8.73 at
0.040-0.050, spread 0-1 bin). The band is one inflow step wide; β0 falls 67 -> 31 -> 4 -> 1 and β1 rises 0 -> 0 -> 11 -> 37 ->
87 across it, so the coexistence sits exactly where the spot phase neither holds nor is gone. The homogeneous arm cannot
show this (its F = 0.055 worlds degenerate to q = 1), which is a property of the arm. Positive control: peak position moves
8 -> 11 bins between the extremes, i.e. a response; it is not monotone, and that was written down before the five-world data
existed.

**What it changes.** X70 measured a graded memory (a daughter follows her parent at about half strength, one generation).
In this band the same question becomes discrete: the daughter should take the parent's **branch**, so the next experiment
(X72) reads branch concordance against a nearest-distance surrogate instead of a correlation slope. That is the first
candidate mechanism in this line for heredity that is not diluted. Report:
`reports/2026-11-01-x71-multistability-result.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x71_multistability.json`. Flagged, not counted: the
F = 0.055 split (q = 6 bins, near the box scale).

## 10er. Branch heredity: the readout failed before the measurement did (X72, 01.11, void)

X71 made the next question concrete: at `F = 0.035` the medium holds two wavelengths, so a daughter could take her parent's
**branch** rather than a blurred size. The X70 replication worlds run at exactly that inflow, so the question looked
answerable from existing data without a single new run. It was not.

The branch had to be read from the component log, which has centroids but no local spectrum; the stand-in was the distance to
the nearest neighbouring structure, with the split at 10.4 cells between X71's 12.0- and 8.73-cell branches. The proxy passes
the pre-registered class-population control (59 % wide) and fails the one that matters: median spacing stays at 11.0–11.5
cells while the global wavelength moves between 12.0 cells and a labyrinth, correlation −0.18, spread 0.7 cells against the
3.3-cell difference it would have to resolve. It measures packing, not size.

The numbers the invalid proxy produced are in the aggregate (`l1_field_x72_branch.json`) and would have read as a clean
negative: parent–daughter concordance 0.517 (chance 0.50), surrogate 0.904, difference −0.387 in all five worlds. They are
not a result: the surrogate is measured at the same instant and in the same patch as the daughter, so its 0.90 is spatial
autocorrelation of the proxy, and the parent is measured a snapshot earlier, so the two are not comparable.

**Verdict: void, not negative** — no claim either way. What the next attempt needs: a **local spectrum** in a 48–64 cell
window from fields logged every **50** steps (the existing fields are every 200, which puts the parent's branch and the
daughter's in different transients), ideally a two-mode fit per patch rather than an argmax. The rule that came out of it is
in `docs/design-checks.md` ("a proxy must move with what it stands for") and applies to every stand-in readout in the line.
Report: `reports/2026-11-01-x72-branch-readout-void.md`.

## 10es. Branch heredity, second attempt: the readout works and the branch is not a class (X72b, 01.11, void)

The first attempt (§10er) failed because the readout was a stand-in that never tracked the wavelength. The second writes the
field every 50 steps and reads a 48-cell window spectrum; the instrument now passes its control (median local wavelength vs
global peak position, correlation **0.90–0.99** in 5/5 worlds, local spread ~15 cells). The measurement still cannot be made,
and for a better reason.

In four of five worlds **85–100 %** of classified structures sit on the **wide** branch (12.0 cells), so parent–daughter
concordance and the surrogate's concordance both sit at the ceiling (0.90–1.00 and 0.95–1.00, differences 0.00–0.02). The
registered refusal rule fires on one class above 75 % and the verdict is **void** — but the diagnosis is informative: X71's
two modes coexist **globally** (≥ 20 % of spectral power each) while a local window's argmax almost always picks the dominant
one. The branch is therefore not a discrete per-structure state that a daughter can inherit; it is a **world-level mixture**
of a dominant lattice and a persistent minority component.

**What this corrects in §10eq.** The coexistence is between worlds (different attractors from different starts) plus a
secondary mode inside each world; no world yet shows two branches side by side as equals. The multistability claim stands as
measured on the global spectrum, and its reading narrows: one dominant branch per world, with a minority component.

**What the third attempt needs.** Two band powers per structure (fraction within ±1 bin of 12.0 and of 8.73 cells) instead of
a class; conditioning on the parent's band, so only the 10–25 % minority cases carry information; and roughly ten worlds of
300 events each, because five worlds leave 20–40 informative events per world. Alternatively the question changes: if the
branch is a world property, the measurable quantity is the **switch rate** and what precedes a switch. Report:
`reports/2026-11-01-x72b-branch-class-void.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x72b_branch.json`.

## 10et. The selection differential fails its own null, and X29 closes on that (01.11)

`X29_eas_flow_lenia` sat in the queue as "the meter is written and the data are on disk, never run". The meter had been run
on 31.10; the report stayed in the scratch directory and the numbers were never written up — because they are a failure.

`S = mean(s at birth | survived) − mean(s at birth | died)` is meant to isolate selection from drift. Its control is an arm
where the trait cannot act (`beta = 0`). Measured over twenty runs of 3000 steps: the shadow arm gives **S = +0.148, 10/10
positive, bootstrap [+0.1405, +0.1545]**, the selection arm (`beta = 1.2`) gives −0.060 with 0/4 positive. A meter that
reports +0.148 where nothing can be selected cannot support −0.060 as evidence of selection in the other direction; the
plausible confound is that `s` correlates with size and birth timing, which set lifetime on their own.

A second defect compounds it: the runs contain **two** uids each (~7 events per run), so even a valid differential would have
an error larger than the effect. Both are recorded, the twenty artifacts are now in git (they carry parameters but no code
commit — stated, not papered over), and the item is closed as **answered by failure of the instrument**, not by a series.
Report: `reports/2026-11-01-x29-eas-meter-null.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x37_eas.json`.

**What a valid version needs:** a null that is zero by construction (S inside strata matched on birth step and area at
birth, or `s` randomised at birth within a run), ten or more uids per run, and the two statistics of that meter — the
differential and the EAS accumulation — reported and validated separately, because bundling them is what let a broken `S`
sit unnoticed beside a plausible accumulation curve.

## 10eu. The multistability is stable: five of five worlds still carry both modes at 24 000 steps (X71b, 01.11)

X71 left one question open by construction — it measured the coexistence at a single horizon, and a field that is still
choosing looks the same as a field that has chosen. X71b repeats the boundary cell at `F = 0.035`, `k = 0.06` for
**24 000** steps, five jittered worlds, fields at 6 000 / 12 000 / 18 000 / 24 000, and applies X71's two-mode rule unchanged.

**All five worlds are mixed at every horizon**, and the shares are constant to two decimals: the peak carries 0.23–0.40 of the
power and the strongest second mode 0.21–0.26 at 6 000 and at 24 000 alike. A transient would have shown a trend — the
dominant mode growing, the second fading — and there is none. The pre-registered verdict is **bistable**.

What "mixed" means, plainly: two humps with a broad remainder, not two equal lattices. Each world keeps its own peak
position (q = 8, i.e. 12.0 cells, in three worlds; q = 11, i.e. 8.73 cells, in two) while the minority component sits at the
other branch — which confirms the reading given in §10es: the branch is a world property and the second branch persists as a
minority mode.

What it does not say: the band's width (the horizon varied, not the inflow), and "two attractors" in the dynamical-systems
sense — the measured statement is "stationary shares over four horizons", and "bistable" is the label registered for it. One
box (96), one `k`, one threshold, five worlds.

Report: `reports/2026-11-01-x71b-persistence-result.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x71b_persistence.json`. Next: half-steps
`F = 0.033 / 0.037` for the real width, and X72c for branch heredity on stationary branches.

## 10ev. The band is narrow: mixed at 0.037, not at 0.033 (X71c, 01.11)

The multistable window was known to be at least the sweep step (X71) and stationary in time (X71b). Its **width** was the
sweep's resolution, not a measurement. X71c repeats the boundary protocol at two half-steps, 24 000 steps each:

| inflow | mixed worlds | states | second-mode shares |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.033 | 3/5 | mixed, mixed, single, single, mixed | 0.19–0.23 |
| 0.035 | 5/5 | mixed ×5 | 0.21–0.26 |
| 0.037 | 4/5 | mixed, mixed, single, mixed, mixed | 0.16–0.28 |

By the registered 4-of-5 bar the verdict is **narrow**: the band covers 0.035–0.037, `0.002 < width < 0.005`. The honest
caveat is in the numbers: at 0.033 two worlds sit at second-mode shares of 0.197 and 0.190, a hair under the 0.2 cut, so the
lower edge is **knife-edge at the chosen threshold** — at 0.19 the band would be twice as wide. What is measured is that the
edge lies within a few hundredths of the cut, and X71b (shares constant to two decimals over four horizons) is what makes the
cut defensible: the second mode is not drifting through 0.2, it sits just above or just below.

**Correction, 23:30 — the width depends on the cut.** Re-running the same fields with the second-mode threshold at 0.15,
0.20 and 0.25 gives **wide** (`w >= 0.004`), **narrow** (`0.002 < w < 0.005`) and **one step** (`w <= 0.005`) — same data, same
registered rule, three widths. The shares near the boundary run 0.19-0.28, so a binary cut draws an arbitrary line through a
continuous quantity. What X71c therefore establishes is a **gradient**, not a width: the second-mode share varies smoothly
across the boundary, and any "band width" number is a joint statement about the medium and the cut. The defensible form is
"at a cut of 0.2 the band spans one to two sweep cells"; the next step is to report the share itself per world and cell
instead of a class.

Both statements are needed: the band is narrow but stable. A wide band that decayed would be a transient; a narrow band that
persists 24 000 steps is a state of the medium. Report: `reports/2026-11-01-x71c-band-width-result.md`; aggregate
`l1_field_x71c_width.json`. Next: re-analyse the existing fields with the cut at 0.15 and 0.25 to separate instrument from
medium — no new series needed.

## 10ew. The mixture belongs to the patch, not to the parent (X72c, 02.11)

Third and last attempt at branch heredity, with the instrument the two earlier attempts produced: per-structure **band powers**
(a 48-cell window, `p_wide` at q_win 3–4 = 12-cell period, `p_narrow` at 5–6 = 8.7-cell) instead of an argmax class, and ten
worlds instead of five so the minority cases can be counted.

Ten worlds at `F = 0.035`, **1 715 events**. Spearman(parent `p_wide`, daughter `p_wide`) = **0.853**, permutation p95 = 0.040,
positive in **10/10** worlds — and it is **not** heredity: the nearest **non-parent** structure predicts the daughter better
(concordance 0.843 against the parent's 0.740 on the 415 minority events, difference −0.104). The high correlation is the
patch correlating with itself: parent and daughter are measured in the same 48-cell window 50 steps apart, and within a
snapshot the band power varies across structures with sd **0.06–0.07**, so almost all variance in the readout is *where* the
structure is.

**Correction made after the run, stated because it changes nothing and sharpens everything:** the preregistered control used
the spread of per-snapshot medians over time (0.11–0.18, failing in 10/10 worlds) instead of the spread across structures in a
snapshot (0.060–0.068, passing). The verdict rests on the surrogate comparison, which never depended on that arithmetic.

**The prediction held.** If the mixture belongs to regions, structures in one quadrant should agree more than quadrants agree
with each other. Tested on the same 1 200 snapshots (no new runs): **η² = 0.307 pooled** (0.218-0.542 per world), i.e. about a
third of the variance of `p_wide` is explained by the quadrant — a positive statement where the heredity test was negative. The
partition is coarse (4 quadrants of 24x24 cells), so this is a lower bound on regionality for finer partitions.

**And the regions are historical, not prescribed.** The heterogeneous inflow pattern is identical in every world, so quadrant
q is "the same place" by external conditions across worlds; yet the quadrant medians are 0.541 / 0.519 / 0.522 / 0.523 with
**η² = 0.004** across worlds. Regions are strongly distinguishable *within* a world (0.307) and indistinguishable *between*
worlds — which is a stronger claim than "it is the patch": the patch is not the environment we prescribed, it is the one the
world made for itself, and that is why a daughter cannot inherit it.

**The contrast on one dataset.** Both readouts on the same 1 955 division events: peak **area** gives parent 0.457 against a
nearest non-parent 0.069 (difference **+0.388**, parent higher in **10/10** worlds), while the band **mixture** gives a higher
raw correlation (0.853) that a neighbour beats. So the contrast is not between datasets: on one geometry, one event set and one
surrogate rule, **size is parent-specific and the mixture is not** — one generation of "how big", no generation of "which form".

**What it closes.** Three instruments (spacing proxy, argmax class, band powers) now give the same answer: nothing
parent-specific survives once the patch is controlled. The two-wavelength coexistence is real, stationary and narrow, and it
belongs to **regions of the world**, not to lineages. Together with §10ep the line's statement becomes a contrast: the medium
carries one generation of **size** memory (0.42–0.57, pair-specific) and **no memory of the mixture**. Report:
`reports/2026-11-02-x72c-mixture-is-the-patch.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x72c_branch.json`.

## 10ex. The size memory decays by 0.4 per division: half-life under one generation (X73, 02.11)

X70 measured one step of heredity; X73 follows the genealogy. Spearman(ancestor peak area, descendant peak area) over
re-analysis of the X71c/X70r worlds (no new runs):

| lag | X72c pairs | X72c rho | X70r rho | shuffle p95 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 2 140 | **0.475** | **0.512** | 0.033-0.055 |
| 2 | 1 968 | **0.204** | **0.239** | 0.037-0.052 |
| 3 | 1 776 | **0.072** | 0.121 | 0.040-0.055 |

The registered verdict is **depth >= 2** (lag-2 0.204 against p95 0.037, and >= 0.15 in 7 of 10 worlds), but the result is the
**shape**: the correlation falls by a factor 0.43 and then 0.35, i.e. roughly **0.4 per division**, a **half-life of 0.7-0.8
divisions**. A copying rule would give a flat curve (0.45, 0.45, 0.45); an exponentially forgetting medium gives this.

That distinction is what the thread needs: a heritable state with a half-life below one generation is enough for a daughter to
resemble her mother and not enough for a lineage to accumulate anything. Limitations stated in the report: survival bias
flattens the curve, peak area is a lifetime maximum, one inflow and one box. Report:
`reports/2026-11-02-x73-depth-result.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x73_depth.json`. Next: the half-life should scale with how
strongly the field pulls structures to its preferred size — a measurable prediction, but it needs worlds with long chains,
i.e. a new run.

## 10ey. The half-life depends on the inflow, and the multistable band is where memory dies fastest (X74, 02.11)

Two new inflow cells (`F = 0.030` and `0.040`, dense 50-step logging — the X71 logs carry no genealogies at all) joined the
0.035 cell of X73:

| inflow | rho lag 1 / 2 / 3 | lambda | half-life |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 0.507 / 0.304 / 0.198 | 0.625 | **1.47** |
| 0.035 | 0.475 / 0.204 / 0.072 | 0.389 | **0.73** |
| 0.040 | 0.486 / 0.278 / 0.205 | 0.649 | **1.61** |

Lag-1 is essentially the same in all three cells (0.475-0.507), so **how much a daughter resembles her mother does not depend on
the inflow**; what depends on it is **how fast that resemblance is forgotten**, and the dependence is not monotone: a U with its
minimum at 0.035, the multistable cell of §10eq-§10ev. Away from the band memory lasts 1.5-1.6 divisions, inside it 0.73.

**The world that can be two things remembers less.** Two mechanisms are consistent and not separated here: competing pulls at
the boundary (a structure's size is under two preferred scales) or kinematics (more splitting and merging, so a parent's size is
replaced faster). The separating measurement — split rate per structure per 1000 steps in each cell — is available in the same
artifacts.

The registered criterion asked for a *consistent direction*; the spread is there (factor 2.0) but the direction is not, so the
claim is stated narrowly: the rate depends on the inflow, and the dependence is non-monotone.

**The mechanism check refuted both candidates.** Splits per component per 1000 steps rise monotonically with `F`
(0.79 → 1.22 → 4.14) — highest where memory lasts longest — and the area-normalised rate falls monotonically (19 567 → 13 191 →
10 180); the size CV is monotone too (0.54 → 0.96 → 1.40). A U-shaped half-life with three monotone covariates means the cause is
what only the boundary cell has: **two coexisting length scales**. That is consistent with §10eq-§10ev but not established — the
check removed two explanations rather than confirming a third. Report:
`reports/2026-11-02-x74-rate-result.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x74_rate.json`.

## 10ez. At the band edges: one cell shallow, one void — inconclusive, and the pattern is in mixedness (X75, 02.11)

The surviving explanation for §10ey was that memory is short where the medium is **mixed**. X75 measured the depth curve at the
two band edges (50-step logging; X71c's 1000-step logs carry no genealogies):

| cell | rho 1/2/3 | half-life | registered check |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.033 | 0.377 / 0.038 / 0.053 | (0.71) | **void**: rho1 below the 0.40 band |
| 0.037 | 0.456 / 0.192 / 0.039 | **0.56** | passes |

Registered verdict: **inconclusive** — one edge shallow, the other outside its control by 0.023. The band `[0.40, 0.60]` was fixed
in advance from the three X74 cells (0.475-0.507, spread 0.032), so the miss is within the band's own noise; relaxing it after
seeing the data is precisely the move the line forbids, so the cell stays void.

What the five cells now say, ordered by mixedness rather than by `F`: 0.030 (not mixed) 1.47, 0.040 (not mixed) 1.61, 0.035
(mixed 5/5) 0.73, 0.037 (mixed 4/5) **0.56**, 0.033 (mixed 3/5) void with rho1 0.377 — the least mixed cell also has the weakest
initial memory, which is the direction the reading predicts. Not a claim: one edge is unusable.

Next, designed but not run: ten worlds at 0.033 (to tighten rho1), or cells at 0.031 / 0.039, where mixedness should interpolate;
either would test the reading without touching the control band. Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x75-edges-result.md`; aggregate
`l1_field_x75_edges.json`.

## 10fa. Memory is two-valued: short inside the multistable band, long outside it (X76, 02.11)

X75 left one cell void: at `F = 0.033` five worlds gave ρ₁ = 0.377, 0.023 under the registered band. X76 replicated that cell with
**ten** worlds: ρ₁ = **0.447** (inside the band), ρ₂ = 0.098, ρ₃ = 0.040, half-life **0.57**. The void was sampling — doubling the
sample moved ρ₁ by 0.07. The per-world table shows why five were not enough: ρ₁ ranges **0.224-0.561**, only 4 of 10 worlds above
0.45.

With that cell filled, the five cells stop forming a U and become **two levels**:

| cell | mixed (X71c) | half-life |
|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | no | 1.47 |
| 0.033 | 3/5 | 0.57 |
| 0.035 | 5/5 | 0.73 |
| 0.037 | 4/5 | 0.56 |
| 0.040 | no | 1.61 |

Inside or at the edge of the multistable band the half-life is **0.56-0.73** divisions; outside it **1.47-1.61** — a factor of
about 2.5, the same at three inflows inside the band. The mechanism is not split rate and not size dispersion (both monotone in
`F`, §10ey); what the shallow cells share is **two coexisting length scales**. That is a description, not a demonstrated cause:
testing it causally would need an independently imposed second scale. Report:
`reports/2026-11-02-x76-033-replication-result.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x76_033.json`.

## 10fb. The patch had no power: neighbour sizes do not predict a structure's size (X77, 02.11)

X70's spatial control compared a daughter with the **nearest non-parent**; the sharper objection is that the whole **patch**
within a wavelength sets her size, with the parent merely a member of it. X77 tested that on the same event sets, with the patch
mean (radius 12 cells, ancestors excluded) as a regressor:

| dataset | events | rho parent | rho patch mean | rho nearest | beta parent given patch |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| X72c | 1 566 | 0.409 | **0.010** | 0.038 | 0.422 [0.326, 0.531] |
| X70r | 779 | 0.438 | **0.007** | 0.067 | 0.441 [0.288, 0.602] |

The registered rule required the control to have power (`rho_patch >= 0.20`) before the coefficient could be read; it does not, and
the median patch holds only two structures. Verdict: **void as a discriminator** — a regressor unrelated to the outcome cannot
absorb anything.

The void is the finding: reading (a) of X70 ("the parent is just a member of a patch whose size sets the daughter") is now
**excluded by measurement**, not argument — neighbour sizes are unrelated to a structure's size in this world (0.01). Together with
X70's local-field test (field richness predicts the daughter at −0.022, the parent at 0.111) both measurable families of confounds
are rejected: neighbour sizes and local richness do not predict a structure's size; her parent does.

Unmeasured: patches much larger than a wavelength (radius 12 was fixed); "patch" as a field quantity rather than a mean of
structures (that is X70's control, separate). Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x77-patch-control-result.md`; aggregate
`l1_field_x77_patch.json`.

## 10fc. The causal test failed on its own manipulation (X78, 02.11)

The two-scale reading (§10ey-§10fa) was to be turned into a manipulation: at `F = 0.040`, prescribe the hetero wavelength **matched**
to the medium's own (8.7 cells) versus **mismatched** (12.0), and see whether memory follows the scale rather than `F`.

The matched arm turned out to be **not a patterned world**: one component per world on average, lag-1 0.212 on 255 pairs, `lambda` >
1 (no decay). `--hetero-scale` chooses the wavelength of an inflow *modulation*, not the wavelength the medium patterns at; at 8.7
cells with `amp = 0.5` that modulation destroys the pattern instead of reinforcing it. The pre-registered control caught it (lag-1
left the band) and the series is **void**.

The surviving arm is a useful consistency check: the mismatched cell is an ordinary `0.040` world (6.8 components, half-life
**1.88** against X74's **1.61** for the same cell) — two independent samples of one cell agree within ~15 %, so the depth number is
reproducible across series.

To impose a second scale properly the runner needs a **composite pattern**: two wavelengths summed in `G` before the `F_eff`
multiplication. That is ~40 lines plus an anchor, and then the same design. Until then the causal status of the two-level statement
is: pattern measured, three monotone covariates excluded, mechanism described but **not manipulated**. Report:
`reports/2026-11-02-x78-causal-void.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x78_causal.json`.

## 10fd. An imposed second scale shortens memory by 1.43x: right direction, not the full band effect (X78b, 02.11)

X78 failed because prescribing a single wavelength at `F = 0.040` killed the field. The runner therefore gained a **composite
pattern** (`--hetero-scale2`, `--hetero-mix2`; off by default, anchored bit-for-bit), and X78b applies it at the robust `0.030`
cell, where the single-scale memory lasts 1.47 divisions.

Manipulation check first: the world stays patterned (**30.6 components**), genealogies are plentiful (**1 464 / 1 438 / 1 394**
pairs at lags 1-3), and **9 of 10** worlds carry two spectral peaks (near q = 8 and q = 11, i.e. 12.0 and 8.7 cells). The composite
reached the world.

| cell | rho 1/2/3 | half-life |
|---|---|---|
| 0.030 single scale | 0.507 / 0.304 / 0.198 | **1.47** |
| 0.030 two imposed scales | 0.498 / 0.228 / 0.129 | **1.03** |

Lag-1 is unchanged (0.498), the decay is steeper, and the half-life falls by a factor **1.43**. The registered rule wanted below 1.0
*and* a factor >= 1.3: the factor is met, the level misses by 0.03, so the verdict is **inconclusive** — reported as it stands.

The narrow reading: an imposed second scale moves memory **in the predicted direction by about a third**, but not to the level of the
medium's own multistability (0.56-0.73). Either a prescribed scale is a weaker perturbation than two genuinely competing attractors,
or the band's shallowness is partly about sitting near a phase boundary — not separated here. For the write-up: imposing a second
scale shortens the half-life by 1.4x, the medium's own band by 2.1-2.5x. Report:
`reports/2026-11-02-x78b-two-scale-result.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x78b_causal.json`.

## 10fe. The mix sweep was confounded by its own construction, and the fix is two lines (X79, 02.11)

X79 swept the weight of the imposed second scale (`--hetero-mix2` 0.25 / 0.75) at `F = 0.030` to separate a graded effect from
saturation. The points are not monotone — 0.76 (w = 0.25), 1.03 (w = 0.50), 0.96 (w = 0.75) — and the registered verdict is
**inconclusive**. The reason is a design error that is worth more than the sweep:

`G = (1-w) G1 + w G2` with independent components has variance `(1-w)^2 + w^2`, **minimal at w = 0.5** (amplitude factor 0.71 against
0.79 at both 0.25 and 0.75). The sweep therefore moved two things at once — the dominant scale *and* the modulation strength — and the
non-monotonicity is what that confound predicts. The two-peak check makes it visible: at w = 0.75 no world shows two peaks (0/10), so
that cell is effectively single-scale at a different wavelength, not two-scale.

What survives: the **w = 0.25** cell is genuinely two-peaked (10/10) and is the shortest-lived long-inflow world measured (0.76 against
1.47 single-scale) — a second point in X78b's direction, with a stronger effect. Fix before the next attempt: renormalise after mixing
(`G <- (G - 0.5)/sigma(w) + 0.5`) so every weight has the same amplitude and only the spectrum changes. Report:
`reports/2026-11-02-x79-mix-confound.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x79_mix.json`.

## 10ff. With amplitude fixed the sweep still cannot separate mixedness from wavelength, and the half-life turns out to be aggregation-dependent (X80, 02.11)

X80 ran the amplitude-normalised composite (`--hetero-mix2` 0.25 / 0.50 / 0.75, five jittered worlds each, `F = 0.030`, field at
6000). All three cells are patterned and powered (27-39 components, 665-981 lag-1 pairs). The registered rule fires **slope**
(0.86 < 1.18 < 1.25, spread 0.39 >= 0.20), and that verdict does not stand, for reasons written into the preregistration:

- the **positive control did not fire** — the plan required both wings above 1.0 and `w = 0.25` gave 0.86;
- the middle cell is **not uniquely two-peaked**: 5/5 at `w = 0.25`, 5/5 at `w = 0.50`, **0/5** at `w = 0.75`. The tie passed a
  clause written for "highest", so the sweep is not three degrees of mixedness but *mixture, mixture, one single scale at 8.7
  cells*.

What the pooled numbers do support: both mixture cells are shorter-lived than the single-scale reference at the same inflow
(0.86 and 1.18 against 1.47) — third and fourth points in X78b's and X79's direction — with **no ordering between them**.

The larger finding is about the ruler. `lambda` is fitted to the ratios of the **pooled** pair pool; per-world it is a different
question, and in X80 the two answers have different signs:

| reading | w = 0.25 | w = 0.50 | w = 0.75 | shape |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| pooled `rho1` | 0.509 | **0.391** | 0.499 | dip |
| pooled half-life | 0.86 | 1.18 | 1.25 | slope |
| per-world median half-life | 1.04 | **0.52** | 1.16 | dip |

The reason is arithmetic, not noise: when `rho1` falls and `rho2`/`rho3` do not fall as fast, `rho2/rho1` **rises**, so `lambda`
rises and the fitted half-life lengthens although the memory starts lower. Half-life is a ratio, not a level.

Recomputed on the stored per-world correlations (no new runs), the same audit on the published cells:

| cell | pooled (published) | per-world median | range | worlds fittable |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F = 0.030 | 1.47 | 1.26 | 1.21-2.44 | 5/5 |
| F = 0.035 | 0.73 | 0.92 | 0.27-3.09 | 10/10 |
| F = 0.040 | 1.61 | 1.82 | 0.82-2.10 | 5/5 |
| F = 0.033 | 0.57 | 0.66 | 0.43-1.86 | **7/10** |
| X79 w = 0.25 | 0.76 | 0.64 | 0.42-1.86 | 9/10 |
| X79 w = 0.75 | 0.96 | 0.89 | 0.21-3.36 | 10/10 |

The **direction** of the multistable band survives (1.26 and 1.82 outside, 0.66 and 0.92 inside), the **size** does not: the
pooled contrast is 2.01x and 2.58x, the per-world median contrast 1.37x and 1.91x. The "two-valued, 2.1-2.5x" sentence of §10fa
and claim 295 is therefore a pooled number and is quoted as one. At `F = 0.033` three of ten worlds have a lag-2 or lag-3
correlation at or below zero, so their `lambda` does not exist at all and the published 0.57 is fitted from a pool that contains
them.

Two gates are now known to be too weak: the two-peak check passes on a tie, and a failed positive control did not void the
series because the plan stated the consequence but the refusal rule did not encode it. Both are fixed in the next registration.

**Registered next (X81), before any run.** Report `rho1` as the primary level and the half-life as a secondary derived quantity,
always printing the per-world median and range beside the pooled value; fit `lambda` per world and aggregate by median, and
declare a cell unfittable if fewer than 7 of 10 worlds yield a positive decay. Replace the mixture with a **prescribed
spectrum**: dominant at q = 8, second peak at q = 11, share swept 0 / 0.5 / 1.0 of the dominant power, random phases,
amplitude-normalised; the manipulation check is the **measured** share from the saved field (void if it deviates from the
prescribed share by more than 0.15, and void on a two-peak tie). Prediction to be falsified: `rho1` falls monotonically with the
measured share of the second peak; if instead the effect tracks the *wavelength* of the second peak at fixed share, mixedness is
not the cause. Ten worlds per cell, 6000 steps. Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x80-dip-estimator.md`; aggregate
`l1_field_x80_dip.json`.

**Correction (02.11, 03:55).** The two-peak count above was a **strict local-maximum** test, and it fails on a shoulder. Measured as
a power share over the same fifteen fields, the three X80 cells carry `P2/(P1+P2) = 0.27-0.31`, `0.18-0.23` and `0.27-0.34`: all
three are two-peaked, and the sweep moved the *prescribed* composition while the *realised* spectrum hardly moved. The "0/5 at
`w = 0.75`" figure is therefore withdrawn, the registered "slope" compares three near-identical spectra, and X80's verdict is **void
by manipulation reach**, not slope. Three pilots then measured the channel directly: prescribed power ratio 1.0 gives realised 0.19,
4.0 gives 0.31, 16.0 gives 0.28 — the second peak saturates at about a third whatever is prescribed. X81 as registered would have
been void by its own check, so it is superseded by **X82** (the transfer function measured with worlds, five cells x five worlds).
Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x80-correction-two-peak.md`; registration: `reports/2026-11-02-x82-reach-plan.md`.

## 10fg. One period and two symmetries: X71's "two wavelengths" was a radial binning artefact (X84, 02.11)

Every spectral number in this line — X71's modes, X78b's two-peak check, X79's and X80's shares — comes from a **radial** profile,
power binned by `|k|` alone. The forcing is a checkerboard, `G = (sin(qy)cos(qx)+1)/2`, whose power sits on the four **diagonals**
`(±q, ±q)`: for the 12-cell pattern `|k| = 8√2 = 11.3`, radial bin 11. A stripe morphology of period 8.7 cells sits at `(±11, 0)` and
`(0, ±11)`, `|k| = 11.0` — also bin 11. **Two different things in one bin, and one of them is the forcing itself.** A control world
with no imposed second scale shows it directly: its four largest peaks are the diagonals at `|k| = 11.3` (63 123 and 57 209) and its
next four are the axis peaks at `|k| = 8.0` (35 612 and 35 431).

Re-read of the fields X71, X71b and X71c already saved (65 snapshots, **no new runs**), with `q'` = axis-aligned wavenumber of maximum
power and `s = P_diag/(P_diag+P_axis)`:

| `F` | `q'` per world | wavelength, cells | `s` median | `s` range |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.025 | 6, 8, 8, 7, 8 | 16.0–12.0 | 0.381 | 0.352–0.598 |
| 0.030 | 8, 8, 9, 9, 8 | 12.0–10.7 | 0.516 | 0.462–0.739 |
| 0.035 | 8, 7, 8, 7, 8 | 12.0–13.7 | 0.777 | 0.643–0.811 |
| 0.040 | 8, 7, 7, 7, 7 | 13.7–12.0 | 0.919 | 0.918–0.948 |
| 0.045 | 8, 7, 7, 7, 7 | 13.7–12.0 | 0.925 | 0.913–0.955 |
| 0.050 | 8, 7, 7, 7, 7 | 13.7–12.0 | 0.924 | 0.908–0.944 |
| 0.055 | 8, 8, 5, 7, 8 | 19.2–12.0 | 0.888 | 0.770–0.934 |

- the period **never splits**: `q'` = 5–9 (10.7–19.2 cells) and never 11, the value an 8.7-cell branch would need;
- the **unforced arm** agrees on the period (`q'` = 7–10) and stays low on symmetry (0.02–0.33); its 0.055 cell is dead (field std 0.0)
  and its 0.050 cell weak (0.035), so the top of that column is a decay, not a second scale;
- Spearman(`s`, `F`) = **0.687** over the 35 forced worlds, and Spearman(`s`, X71's radial `q_peak`) = **0.729** — the radial "mode" is
  the symmetry;
- X71b's "coexistence stable over 24 000 steps" is `s` = 0.777 → 0.775 over four horizons: flat because the forcing is fixed;
- X71c's "band width" is the width of a smooth crossover — `s` = 0.663 at `F = 0.033` and 0.901 at 0.037 — so any cut reads a band.

What replaces the old picture: **one period (~12 cells, set by the medium, the same with and without forcing) and one symmetry
coordinate `s ∈ [0,1]` that rises from ~0.4 at `F = 0.025` to a plateau ~0.92 at `F ≥ 0.040`.** The environment can impose
**orientation** at the medium's own scale; it cannot move the scale. For the life question this is the cleaner handle: what a
two-scale world was supposed to test (does imposed structure shorten memory) becomes "does the symmetry carry memory", and the
readout for that already exists on stored fields. Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x84-one-period-two-symmetries.md`; aggregate
`l1_field_x84_angular.json`. Registered plan (deviation stated in the report): `reports/2026-11-02-x84-angular-plan.md`.

## 10fh. The reach test voided itself and confirmed the period: sixteen times the power moves nothing (X82, 02.11)

X82 ran five cells (prescribed second-line power ratio 0, 1, 4, 16 at `q = 11`, and 4 at `q = 14`), five worlds each, to measure the
transfer function from prescribed to realised spectral share. **It is void by its own control**: the single-scale cell gives a radial
share of **0.304** against a required below-0.15, and X84 explains why — the checkerboard forcing line of the 12-cell pattern sits at
`|k| = 8√2 = 11.3`, inside the `[10, 13]` window, in every world. The registered quantity was measuring the forcing against the
structure band, so no claim is built on it.

The angular readout carried alongside the aggregate is interpretable and consistent:

| cell | second line | `q'` | symmetry `s` | `rho1` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| share 0 | — | 9, 8, 9, 9, 8 | 0.603 | 0.565 |
| share 1 | `q = 11` | 8, 6, 7, 6, 7 | 0.652 | 0.328 |
| share 4 | `q = 11` | 8, 6, 6, 7, 7 | 0.494 | 0.423 |
| share 16 | `q = 11` | 7, 8, 7, 7, 7 | **0.324** | 0.507 |
| share 4 | `q = 14` | 8, 8, 8, 8, 8 | 0.710 | 0.572 |

- the **period never moves**: `q'` = 6–9 (10.7–16.0 cells) in every cell, including the one whose second line sits at 6.9 cells. A
  second line at sixteen times the power of the first buys no change of period — the fifth confirmation of §10fg, now with a
  manipulation that is far outside the medium's band;
- an **in-band** second line breaks the lock (s 0.603 → 0.652 → 0.494 → 0.324), while an **off-band** one at the same prescribed power
  leaves it intact (0.710). So `s` is not only a function of inflow: it measures how strongly the forcing is one coherent
  checkerboard;
- `rho1` drops in exactly the two disturbed cells (0.328 and 0.423 against 0.565–0.572), which is the question X85 was registered to
  test with proper power — these five-world numbers are descriptive only.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x82-reach-void.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x82_reach.json`. Registration (void, kept as written):
`reports/2026-11-02-x82-reach-plan.md`.

## 10fi. Symmetry against memory voided itself, and found that the logging frequency is a physical parameter (X85, 02.11)

X85 asked whether the size memory depends on the symmetry `s` — the one knob X84 showed the environment can turn. Three inflows,
eight worlds each, 24 runs. **Void by its own positive control**: the `F = 0.035` cell was required to sit in `[0.64, 0.81]` (X71's
0.777) and gave **0.870** (0.778–0.901).

The reason is not seed scatter. X71 ran at `--sample-every 1000`; X85 runs at 50, and in `field_track.py` the trait mutations are
drawn **at every sampled step** while the trait feeds back into the field. X71's worlds saw six mutation events over 6000 steps,
X85's saw a hundred and twenty. Verified directly: seed 191 at `F = 0.035`, identical flags except logging, gives fields that differ
by up to **0.365**. Different logging density is a different world, so the borrowed control band was invalid — and, more importantly,
**every cross-series comparison in this line must state its logging regime**. X71/X71b/X71c used 1000; X73–X85 used 50.

Within the 50-step regime the direction replicates: X85 gives `s` = 0.399 → 0.671 → 0.870 for `F` = 0.025 → 0.030 → 0.035 (ranges
0.357–0.474, 0.503–0.808, 0.778–0.901), and X82's control cell independently gave 0.603 at 0.030. Across regimes the direction still
replicates (X71: 0.38 → 0.52 → 0.78) but the **level** does not: 0.516 (1000-step) against 0.603/0.671 (50-step) at `F = 0.030`.

The substantive numbers, descriptive only: within-cell Spearman(`rho1`, `s`) = **−0.33, −0.25, −0.02**; pooled raw correlation
**+0.26**; **partial correlation controlling inflow 0.00**. So the registered prediction (memory shorter where the pattern is locked to
the forcing) appears in two cells below the bar and vanishes once inflow is held — the hypothesis is neither supported nor refuted, and
the instrument is the binding constraint. Placing a cell's `s` to ±0.03 needs about twenty worlds, 60 runs for the curve.

Reports: `reports/2026-11-02-x85-symmetry-memory-void.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x85_symmetry_memory.json`. Rule added to
`docs/design-checks.md` ("logging frequency is a physical parameter").

## 10fj. The symmetry curve is placed, and the identity convention turns out to limit which questions a series can be asked (X86, 02.11)

X86 ran sixty worlds — twenty per inflow, all at `--sample-every 50` (the X82/X85 regime) — to place `s(F)` at the precision the line
now needs.

| `F` | `s` median | IQR | sd | SE | axis period `q'` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.025 | **0.421** | 0.365–0.609 | 0.138 | 0.031 | 4–8 |
| 0.030 | **0.674** | 0.519–0.762 | 0.163 | 0.036 | 8–10 |
| 0.035 | **0.848** | 0.778–0.880 | 0.110 | 0.025 | 7–9 |

The registered control fired (ordered medians, 0.025→0.035 gap **0.427** against a required 0.15) and both adjacent steps clear the
declared resolution: **+0.253** (5.3σ) and **+0.174** (3.9σ). The curve is **placed**. The 0.030 value, 0.674, reproduces X85's 0.671
measured on eight different worlds to three hundredths — the first clean replicate of a level in this line.

Two corrections to the plan's own arithmetic, stated rather than hidden: the observed `sd(s)` is 0.110–0.163, not the 0.07–0.10 that
X85's eight worlds suggested, so the standard error is 0.025–0.036 and the resolution ≈0.08 rather than 0.05 (both steps still clear
it); and `q'` spans 4–10 bins (9.6–24 cells), coarser at `F = 0.025`, so "one period ≈ 12 cells" is a median with real spread — never
11, which is the part X84 refuted.

**The secondary readout does not exist.** The plan expected these worlds to serve the heredity question later "without a new run". They
cannot: with `--split-new-uid` off (X71's convention, chosen so the symmetry numbers match X71's) the identity tracker keeps **4–7
uids** and `uid_pairs` is **empty**. X71 has the same 0 pairs, which is why it never measured heredity, while the split convention
gives 209–250 pairs per run (X74, X85). The registered refusal rule required ≥400 pairs and would have voided the series over a
readout it could not produce; the aggregate separates the two, judges the curve on its own gate and marks the second unavailable with
its cause.

So the lesson repeats in a second costume: `--sample-every` and `--split-new-uid` are not conveniences but parameters that decide which
questions a series can be asked. A design names, before the run, every readout the worlds will serve.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x86-curve-placed.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x86_symmetry_curve.json`. Registered next: the memory-versus-
symmetry test on worlds that carry both readouts (X85's eight per cell plus twelve more, split on, 36 runs).

## 10fk. Orientation does not carry the memory, and the U-shaped band does not survive twenty worlds (X87, 02.11)

X87 brought X85's cells to twenty worlds each (twelve new per inflow, seeds 221–232, flags verified identical), so the memory and the
symmetry are measured on the same worlds at last.

| `F` | `rho1` | pairs | ρ(`rho1`, `s`) within cell | `s` median | half-life, per world | fittable |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.025 | 0.391 | 4 901 | **−0.05** | 0.385 | **1.82** | 20/20 |
| 0.030 | 0.549 | 4 791 | **−0.26** | 0.679 | **1.16** | 18/20 |
| 0.035 | 0.470 | 4 115 | **−0.21** | 0.837 | **1.07** | 19/20 |

The registered control fired (0.030 `rho1` 0.549 in [0.45, 0.56]; 0.035 symmetry 0.837 in [0.78, 0.90]) and the verdict is
**symmetry does not carry the memory** — all three coefficients inside ±0.3, pooled partial correlation controlling inflow **−0.10**,
raw pooled correlation +0.16 being inflow confounding (`s` tracks `F` at 0.90). At twenty worlds a coefficient of 0.26 is ≈1.2σ, so the
bound is: a negative association stronger than ≈0.3 is excluded, nothing smaller.

What the environment can turn — orientation — therefore does not carry the state that the heredity numbers measure. Memory belongs to
the medium, not to the symmetry it displays. That closes the question the two-scale programme was built to ask.

**And the U-shaped band does not survive.** Per-world median half-life is **1.82 / 1.16 / 1.07** at 0.025 / 0.030 / 0.035: monotone
declining, not U-shaped. §10fa and claim 295 described a U (1.47 / 0.73 / 1.61) from five worlds and pooled pairs; the 0.025 inflow was
never measured then, and the 0.035 cell moved 0.73 → 0.85 → 1.07 as the sample grew from five to eight to twenty. Direction: memory
shortens as inflow rises in the range where the medium is single-scale. The high-inflow arm (0.040–0.050), where the morphology
percolates, remains unmeasured at this power — that is the only place a U could still live, and it is what to run next (~60 runs).

Reports: `reports/2026-11-02-x87-symmetry-does-not-carry-memory.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x87_memory_vs_symmetry.json`.

## 10fl. The U is real, and above the band heredity stops existing (X88, 02.11)

> **17:45 — read this with §10fs.** Every half-life below is a function of peak area, and peak area is defined on `v > 0.20`. X99 moved
> the cut to 0.15 and 0.25: at `F = 0.030` the half-life ran 0.77 / 1.16 / 2.26 and at 0.040 1.84 / 2.08 / 0.69, so the **levels move by a
> factor of three and the ordering between inflows reverses at 0.25**. The numbers here are correct at their cut and must be quoted with
> it; the U is a property of the 0.20 definition as much as of the world.

X88 measured the one arm never measured at power — `F = 0.040 / 0.045 / 0.050`, twenty worlds each, flags identical to X87.

| `F` | half-life, per-world median | ρ₁ | pairs | fittable | components |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.025 (X87) | 1.82 | 0.391 | 4 901 | 20/20 | lattice |
| 0.030 (X87) | 1.16 | 0.549 | 4 791 | 18/20 | lattice |
| 0.035 (X87) | **1.07** | 0.470 | 4 115 | 19/20 | lattice |
| 0.040 | **2.08** | 0.471 | 3 753 | 20/20 | 6.9 |
| 0.045 | **1.60** | 0.165 | 636 | 17/20 | 1.1 |
| 0.050 | **undefined** | 0.0 | 6 | 0/20 | 1.0 |

Registered verdict: **void**, on the pair-count clause — the 0.050 cell has six pairs against a floor of 400. But that void is the world:
at 0.050 the pattern is one component in all twenty worlds, nothing divides, and there are no lineages. Heredity is undefined there, not
unmeasured.

What the measurable cells say: the curve is a **U with its minimum at 0.035**, 1.82 → 1.16 → 1.07 → 2.08 → 1.60. Memory is longest far
from the band (0.025), shortest at the band's edge (0.035), long again once the pattern percolates (0.040). The control fired (0.040:
`ρ₁` 0.471 in [0.42, 0.55], half-life 2.08 in [1.2, 2.3]), and β₁ > 0 in all sixty worlds.

Consequences, stated as corrections rather than quietly: the **two-level direction of §10fa is restored**, but its values are not —
inside 1.07–1.16 (not 0.56–0.73), outside 1.82–2.08 (not 1.47–1.61), i.e. about 1.5× larger on both sides, because those numbers were
pooled pairs at five worlds. And the monotone reading I entered at 07:45 in §10fk is **withdrawn**: the decline holds only from 0.025
to 0.035, and it is the left branch of a U, not the whole curve. The minimum is a plateau: 0.030 and 0.035 differ by 0.09, inside the
±0.13 standard error.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x88-u-restored.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x88_percolating_arm.json`. Open: where exactly between 0.045 and
0.050 the lineages vanish (636 → 6 pairs over a half-step), and where the minimum actually sits (needs ~50 worlds per cell).

## 10fm. Lineages do not switch off, they fade: a factor 2.4 per half-step (X89, 02.11)

X88 left the sharpest edge in the line unexplained: 636 ancestor-descendant pairs at F = 0.045, six at 0.050. X89 put four half-steps
between them (eight worlds each, seeds 261-268, flags identical).

| F | pairs per world (median, range) | single-component worlds | beta1 > 0 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.045 (X88) | 31.8 | 0/20 | - |
| 0.046 | **13.0** (8-19) | 8/8 | 8/8 |
| 0.047 | **6.0** (4-11) | 8/8 | 8/8 |
| 0.048 | **2.0** (0-2) | 8/8 | 8/8 |
| 0.049 | **1.0** (0-2) | 8/8 | 8/8 |
| 0.050 (X88) | 0.3 | 20/20 | 20/20 |

Registered verdict: **graded disappearance** — the fall is monotone and no single half-step reaches the 10x bar; the steps are 2.4x,
2.2x, 3.0x, 2.0x, 3.3x. The control fired (0.046 at 13 pairs per world against a floor of 10).

Two things are worth naming. First, the world is already a maze at 0.046 — every one of the 32 new worlds is a single component with
holes — and it **still divides**, thirteen times per world at 0.046 and once at 0.049. What disappears across this range is the
**rate** of division, not the ability. Second, the decay is geometric in inflow: a factor 2.4 per 0.001 of F, i.e. the lineage count
halves roughly every 0.0008.

So the boundary heredity work needs is a slope, not a wall: the *measurable* edge (four hundred pairs, what an estimate needs) sits near
F = 0.046, while the *phenomenological* edge (no divisions at all) is above 0.050 or does not exist. A medium whose divisions become
arbitrarily rare is not a medium without inheritance — it is a medium where inheritance is not observable, and the write-up must keep
those apart. For the life question this is a quantitative shape: the rate at which a medium creates new individuals is a continuous
function of its position in the parameter plane.

Report: 
eports/2026-11-02-x89-lineages-fade.md; aggregate l1_field_x89_lineage_edge.json. Open: whether the trickle reaches zero
above 0.050 (needs a longer horizon or a bigger box), and whether the lattice-to-maze melt and the fading of divisions are one
transition or two (same scan at another k).

## 10fn. Above 0.049 the medium stops dividing in practice, and the control that says so voids itself (X90, 02.11)

X89 left the lineage count at 0.3 pairs per world at `F = 0.050`, at the resolution limit. X90 doubled the horizon to 12 000 steps in
three cells (eight worlds each) to separate "rare" from "stopped".

Registered verdict: **void**. The control required the 0.050 cell to reach a median of one event per world so that a ratio could be
taken; it reached **0**. A ratio against a median of zero does not exist.

| `F` | pairs per world at 6 000 | at 12 000 | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.048 | 2.0 | 2.0 (0–4) | 1.0 |
| 0.049 | 1.0 | 2.0 (0–2) | 2.0 |
| 0.050 | 0.3 | 0.0 (0–2) | — |

What this does say, descriptively: at 0.049 the count doubles with the horizon, so reproduction there is rare but continuing; at 0.048 it
does not, so that world's divisions are a **transient** finished inside the first 6 000 steps; at 0.050 the median world never divides at
all over 12 000 steps (six events across eight worlds). The correct shape is therefore not a rate decaying smoothly to zero but a
**transient whose duration shrinks with inflow** — and above 0.049 it is over before it produces anything countable.

The design error is mine and worth naming: a control written as "median at least one event" is satisfied only where there is something
to compare, so the cell where the question matters is the cell that voids the series. The right instrument for a last trickle is a rate
over a long horizon with enough worlds; at 0.75 events per world over eight worlds the Poisson uncertainty on the cell mean (±0.3) is the
size of the whole effect. A decisive answer would need ~50 000 steps (four GPU-hours for one cell) or a bigger box, and it is not worth
it for the heredity line, which needs hundreds of pairs and has none at 0.050 at any sane horizon.

So the statement for the write-up is "above 0.049 the medium stops dividing **in practice**" — a bound, not a rate, and with the words
*in practice* doing real work. The three cells remain the same regime as X89: single component and β₁ > 0 in all 24 worlds, so the maze
is alive and simply no longer divides.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x90-trickle-void.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x90_trickle.json`.

## 10fo. The shoulders' memory is lineage memory, and one cell cannot host the control (X91, 02.11)

The map of §10fl says the medium is habitable at two shoulders of the band (F = 0.025, lattice; F = 0.040, just above the percolation
threshold) and forgets quickly in the middle. The way that could have been an artefact is the same one X70 caught earlier: a daughter's
size may follow the **place** she was born rather than her mother. X91 ran the parent-versus-nearest-non-ancestor control (the code and
convention of X73) on the stored artifacts — X87's 60 worlds and X88's 40 — with no new runs.

| F | events | neighbour found | rho parent | rho nearest non-ancestor | gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.025 | 3 001 | 96 % | 0.400 | 0.104 | **0.296** |
| 0.030 | 3 054 | 96 % | 0.550 | 0.236 | **0.314** |
| 0.035 | 2 403 | 91 % | 0.475 | 0.016 | **0.459** |
| 0.040 | 3 753 | 84 % | 0.471 | 0.064 | **0.407** |
| 0.045 | 636 | 49 % | 0.165 | -0.585 | (control not applicable) |

The positive control fired: at 0.035 the parent correlation is 0.475 against X73's 0.457 (band [0.35, 0.55]), same code, twenty worlds
instead of ten. In every cell where the control is possible the gap is 0.30-0.46, **including both shoulders** — so the long half-life
there is specific to the line, not a region resembling itself.

Registered verdict: **void**, because the 0.045 cell finds a neighbour for only 49 % of its events (the world is one component and has
almost no non-ancestors). The rule voided the whole series for a defect of one cell; that is corrected here to a **cell-level** void and
written into the design rules: a control that needs a population cannot be applied where there is no population, and the cell — not the
series — is what becomes uninterpretable.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x91-lineage-specific.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x91_lineage_specificity.json`. What is still missing for a
claim about inheritance in the biological sense is the third leg: whether the resemblance **pays**, i.e. whether a lineage whose size
matches its parent's leaves more descendants. X29 tried and its meter was invalid; the field line still has no measurement of consequence.

## 10fp. Size is not a currency in this medium: weakly good in the lattice, bad in the maze, and the tempting correlation is mechanical (X92, 02.11)

X91 closed the artefact route for resemblance; the remaining leg is consequence — does a structure's size buy it persistence or descendants?
X29 failed with an invalid meter (selection differential +0.148 where the trait cannot act), so X92 tried the stored artifacts: every uid's
peak area against its lifetime, and against its number of daughters.

| F | uids | rho(area, lifetime) | rho(area, daughters) | partial (lifetime held) | null p95 | median lifetime |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.025 | 3 090 | **+0.185** | 0.843 | 0.860 | 0.035 | 424 |
| 0.030 | 3 136 | **+0.110** | 0.806 | 0.834 | 0.036 | 307 |
| 0.035 | 2 486 | **-0.074** | 0.656 | 0.667 | 0.039 | 222 |
| 0.040 | 3 932 | **-0.046** | 0.624 | 0.632 | 0.031 | 89 |

Registered verdict: **mixed** — only the daughters readout clears the 0.10 bar.

Two readings, and only one of them can be trusted. **Lifetime flips sign with morphology**: a bigger structure lives a little longer in
the lattice (+0.19, +0.11) and a little *shorter* in the percolating arm (-0.07, -0.05), consistent with a maze in which the largest
components are the thickenings about to be cut. Median lifetime collapses across the same range, 424 steps to 89. **The daughters
correlation is mechanical**: with the split identity convention, a component that divides is by construction the large one, so area and
out-degree share an object; shuffling areas breaks the pairing but not that link, which is why a null of 0.031-0.039 sits under a
coefficient of 0.62-0.86 and settles nothing.

So the field line still has **no measurement of consequence**. What it now has is a warning and a design: the clean form is a **sibling
contrast** — among daughters of the same mother, does the larger sister leave more daughters? That holds the mother, the place and the
moment fixed and cannot be produced by the mechanical link. The stored pairs define siblings; following a sister's own descendants needs
a longer horizon or a denser log, about one GPU-hour.

Reports: `reports/2026-11-02-x92-pay-mixed.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x92_pay.json`.

## 10fq. Sisters: a small advantage in the lattice, nothing at the edge, a penalty in the maze (X93, 02.11)

The third leg of the heredity claim is consequence, and the two earlier attempts failed for opposite reasons — X29's meter was invalid,
X92's correlation was mechanical. The clean form is a within-family contrast: daughters of the same mother, born at the same moment, so the
mother, the place and the time are fixed. The outcome is **lifetime**, which is not mechanically tied to area.

| F | families | sister pairs | larger sister lives longer | cross-family control | difference | z |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.025 | 2 437 | 1 978 | **0.553** | 0.502 | **+0.050** | 4.68 |
| 0.030 | 2 364 | 1 946 | 0.530 | 0.449 | **+0.082** | 2.67 |
| 0.035 | 1 987 | 1 930 | 0.506 | 0.528 | -0.021 | 0.55 |
| 0.040 | 1 708 | 1 982 | **0.401** | 0.422 | -0.021 | **-8.85** |

Registered verdict: **void**, because the cross-family control at 0.040 reads 0.422, outside the [0.45, 0.55] band. The control is right
to fail and the band was wrong: in a maze a larger structure lives shorter even among strangers (X92: -0.046), so a cross-family fraction
near 0.5 was never achievable. The lesson is written into the design rules — a control that assumes a null population-level relation must
be specified as a **difference**, not as an absolute fraction.

Descriptively, with the difference against its own control as the readable quantity: the lattice pays a little (+0.050 at 0.025 with
z = 4.7; +0.082 at 0.030 with z = 2.7 — the first clears every bar the rule asked for, but only one cell does), the band's edge pays
nothing (-0.021), and in the maze the larger sister is the *less* durable one (0.401, z = -8.85 on 1 982 pairs).

So the third leg is answered as far as this instrument can: **resemblance buys a small amount of persistence in the lattice and nothing, or
less than nothing, where the pattern percolates.** Together with X92 this is coherent — in a maze size marks the place about to be cut.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x93-sisters-void.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x93_sibling_contrast.json`. The field line now has a reading of all
three legs, with the consequence leg being the weakest and the only one whose control is still arguable.

## 10fr. The angular map has no threshold, the memory map does — and the check that discovered this voided itself (X98, 02.11)

X71c taught the line that a conclusion can belong to a cut rather than to the world ("the band is 0.002-0.005 wide" turned out to be the
width of a smooth crossover). The map of §10fg-§10fl rests on the same readout family, so X98 asked whether it survives a different
excursion threshold, four cuts on stored fields.

Registered verdict: **void**, because the positive control fired against the check: at the 0.20 cut the recomputed medians missed the
committed ones by 0.02-0.07 in all four cells. The reason is a category error in the design and worth recording: `q'` and `s` are
computed from the **continuous field**, while the design computed them from a **masked** field, and multiplying by a binary mask
convolves the spectrum with a window function. The four "thresholds" were therefore four different statistics, not four cuts of one.

So: **the angular map has no threshold** — the cut does not enter it. Running the four variants anyway, the ordering of the cells survives
in all of them (0.53/0.76/0.88/0.95 at the 0.15 variant up to 0.64/0.76/0.89/0.96 at 0.30, gaps above 0.05 everywhere) and `q' = 11`
appears only as an occasional world, never as a cell median — weak corroboration, not a control.

**What is genuinely threshold-dependent and untested is the memory map.** Peak areas are defined on `v > 0.2`, and every half-life in
§10ff-§10fl comes from them; the stored artifacts cannot be re-derived at another cut because the genealogies were built at 0.2 during the
run. That is the reviewer's obvious question and it has no answer today. Registered as **X99**: two cells at `--threshold 0.15` and
`0.25`, twenty worlds each (40 runs, ~1 h), waiting on nobody.

Reports: `reports/2026-11-02-x98-threshold-void.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x98_threshold.json`.

## 10fs. The memory map is definition-bound: three cuts, a factor of three, and a reversal (X99, 02.11)

X98 voided because the angular map has no threshold; X99 asked the question of the map that does have one. Two inflows x two further
excursion cuts x ten worlds, against the committed 0.20 values (1.16 at `F = 0.030`, 2.08 at 0.040).

| cell | cut | half-life | vs 0.20 | rho1 | components |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 0.15 | **0.77** | -0.39 | 0.552 | 43.1 |
| 0.030 | 0.20 | 1.16 | - | - | - |
| 0.030 | 0.25 | **2.26** | **+1.10** | 0.479 | 54.4 |
| 0.040 | 0.15 | **1.84** | -0.24 | 0.392 | 2.5 |
| 0.040 | 0.20 | 2.08 | - | - | - |
| 0.040 | 0.25 | **0.69** | **-1.39** | 0.474 | 23.2 |

Registered verdict: **void**, on a floor that was mine to get right and was not: the rule required at least ten mean components, which a
percolating cell has never had (2.5 at the 0.15 cut is its regime, not a collapse — X88 avoided exactly this mistake with a
"few components **and** beta1 > 0" check).

The finding is legible regardless. **The levels are not robust**: a factor of almost three across the cuts at 0.030 and the same at
0.040. **The ordering between inflows reverses**: at 0.20 and 0.15 the maze remembers longer (2.08 vs 1.16; 1.84 vs 0.77), at 0.25 the
lattice does (2.26 vs 0.69). And the reason is plain — the cut decides which cells count as a structure, so it decides what "size"
means: at 0.15 the maze is 2.5 objects of ~530 cells, at 0.25 it is 23 objects of ~70.

Consequence: claims 305/306 and this section's predecessors stand **at threshold 0.20 and only there**, and every half-life in the line
must be quoted with its estimator and its cut. This is the third family member of the same species met today — pooled vs per-world
(§10ff), logging regime (§10fi), excursion cut here — and the rule that covers all three is: a number that looked like a property of the
world was partly a property of a choice, and the choice goes into the sentence.

Next step is not another cut but a size defined on the continuous field: the integral of `v` over a component's basin, or area at half
maximum. Registered as X100; it can be evaluated on stored fields first, without new runs.

Reports: `reports/2026-11-02-x99-cut-dependent.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x99_threshold_memory.json`.

## 10ft. Heredity lives in the skirt, not in the core (X100, 02.11)

X99 showed the memory map is definition-bound. X100 asked the natural follow-up: does heredity survive a size defined **at each
structure's own half-maximum** instead of at a global cut? Twenty runs at `F = 0.030` and `0.040` with a field saved at every logged step
(`--save-fields-every`, anchored), so each structure is measured at its own peak.

| cell | pairs | basin found | rho1, threshold area 0.20 | rho1, half-max basin integral | rho1, half-max basin area | paired delta |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 2 165 | 97 % | **0.531** | **0.176** | 0.168 | **-0.355** |
| 0.040 | 1 761 | 84 % | **0.384** | **0.058** | 0.059 | **-0.326** |

Registered verdict: **void**, because the control band was taken from the committed twenty-world value and applied to ten new worlds
(whose own standard error is 0.10). The paired comparison does not suffer from that — both definitions are evaluated on the same pairs —
and it is large and consistent.

**The finding is about which part of a structure is inherited.** A basin bounded at half of its own maximum is a **core**: 18 cells
against a threshold component of 76 at 0.030, and 17 against 423 at 0.040. So the two "sizes" measure different objects — the height of a
peak versus how far it spreads — and heredity follows the **spread**. Two details sharpen it: the basin's area is as weak as its integral
(0.168, 0.059), so within the basin it is not mass versus extent that matters; and the effect is the same in a lattice and in a maze,
which makes it a property of the medium rather than of one regime.

That also explains X99 in one sentence: the excursion cut matters so much because it decides **how much of the skirt counts**.

What the plan promised — a size with no threshold at all — was too strong: a boundary relative to each structure is still a definition,
just a different kind. A truly boundary-free version needs basins defined by the field's own topology (watershed on `v`, no level set),
registered as X101; it can run on the snapshots already on disk, without new GPU work.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x100-core-vs-skirt.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x100_cutfree.json`.

## 10fu. Three definitions of size, one ladder: heredity is strongest where the definition is an excursion set (X101, 02.11)

X100 compared two definitions and found the core weaker than the skirt. X101 added the only definition with no level set at all — basins
by **steepest ascent**, each cell assigned to the local maximum it drains to — and evaluated all three on the same ancestor-descendant
pairs (2 165 at `F = 0.030`, 1 761 at 0.040), from the snapshots X100 already saved.

| definition | boundary | rho1 at 0.030 | rho1 at 0.040 | median size (0.030 / 0.040) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| excursion set, `v > 0.20` | an absolute level | **0.531** | **0.384** | 76 / 423 |
| basin by steepest ascent | the field's own topology | 0.300 | 0.174 | 112 / 81 |
| basin at half the local maximum | a level relative to each structure | 0.176 | 0.058 | 18 / 17 |

Registered verdict: **heredity needs the cut** (the basin moved rho1 by 0.231 and 0.209). Both controls fired: the threshold-based rho1
reproduced X100's values within 0.02, and the basins partitioned the field in all twenty worlds.

The three definitions are **not three measurements of one quantity**. The ordering is the same at both inflows and monotone in a single
property: how much the definition behaves like a fixed absolute excursion. The topological basin — which merges structures across saddles
and pools neighbours, 112 cells against 76 — keeps about 60 % of the heredity; the relative core keeps a third or less.

That reading also reconciles the whole day. What the medium passes from mother to daughter is **how far the excursion spreads** — an
extensive property of a fixed-density region. It is not the peak height, and it is not the drainage basin, which is a property of the
field's topography rather than of the structure. On this reading the definition dependence is not a defect of the instrument: the
inherited object *is* the excursion set, and a measure that stops being one stops measuring it. The cost is that every heredity number in
this line is a statement about excursion sets at 0.20, with a known sensitivity of a factor of three in rho1 (X99, X100, X101).

Open, and analysis-only: does the **specificity** of X91 (parent beats nearest non-ancestor by 0.26-0.41) also shrink down the ladder?
If it does, heredity is an excursion-set concept through and through; if it holds, the specificity is robust where the level is not.
Registered as X102.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x101-definition-ladder.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x101_watershed.json`.

## 10fv. The specificity is definition-bound too, but the containment is not (X102, 02.11)

X101's ladder showed the *level* of size heredity falling with the definition. X102 asked whether X91's *specificity* — a daughter
follows her own parent more than her nearest non-ancestor, gap 0.26-0.41 — survives the same weakening. Same daughters, two definitions,
ten worlds per inflow:

| inflow | definition | rho parent | rho stranger | gap |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | excursion set, `v > 0.20` | 0.531 | 0.219 | **0.312** |
| 0.030 | basin by steepest ascent | 0.308 | 0.291 | **0.017** |
| 0.040 | excursion set, `v > 0.20` | 0.384 | 0.169 | **0.215** |
| 0.040 | basin by steepest ascent | 0.153 | 0.163 | **-0.010** |

Registered verdict: **specificity is definition-bound too** (the gap falls by 0.295 and 0.225, past the declared 0.15). At 0.040 the
basin gap is zero to two decimals: under the weaker definition the neighbour is as good a predictor as the mother.

The collapse was checked for the mechanical explanation before being read. If the nearest stranger were almost always inside the
daughter's own drainage basin, the basin area could not separate the candidates by construction. Same-frame containment says otherwise:
a parent's peak site lies in the daughter's basin **0.398 / 0.187** of the time against a stranger's **0.132 / 0.081** — ancestry is
visible in the topography, two to three times the stranger rate. The basin readout had room, so the collapse is a property of the
quantity and not of the control: the basin's *extent* carries no heredity, the basin's *identity* does.

(That paragraph replaces a first, published-nowhere attempt that compared basin labels **across frames**. Labels are numbered per
snapshot, so the parent looked no more often inside than the stranger, 0.13 against 0.07 — the opposite reading. The rule is now in
`docs/design-checks.md`: a label is comparable only inside its own frame.)

Together, level and specificity give one sentence: what the medium passes from mother to daughter is **an extensive property of a fixed
density region** — the width of the skirt, not the size of the valley she sits in, and not the height of her peak. The next test is
topographic and is registered: if the daughter's fate depends on **where** in the mother's basin she sits, this reading is refuted
(X103); if the gap moves with the cut the way the level does (X99), it is an excursion-set quantity twice over (X104).

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x102-specificity-ladder.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x102_specificity_ladder.json`; runs: X100's twenty, no
new GPU.

## 10fw. Where a daughter is born matters in the maze, not in the lattice — and the basin was the wrong instrument (X103–X103d, 02.11)

X102 left two readings: heredity is the width of the excursion (the basin is only the stage), or heredity is topographic (what a
daughter inherits is where she starts inside the valley). Four analyses were needed to answer it, and the first two are voids worth
recording because they priced the instrument.

**X103** asked the question with the mother's basin and voided at coverage 0.599 against a 0.60 gate for 0.040. The denominator
counted every ancestor–daughter pair, including 622 of 1 761 (35 %) whose ancestor or daughter was never logged at all — at that
inflow the median lifetime is 89 steps against a 50-step logging interval, so a structure born and divided between two samples is
invisible. Among pairs whose peaks exist the instrument was available 0.973 and 0.925.

**X103b** fixed the denominator and added a selection control (median daughter area, kept versus dropped, in [0.75, 1.33]). It
voided again, and this time informatively: at 0.030 the 54 pairs lost had daughters of median area 80 against 53 (ratio 0.662). The
basin loses the mothers that sit on structure boundaries — precisely the ones that produce large excursions — so the missing third
is not random and the question had to be asked without the basin.

**X103c** used only the distance between the mother's peak centroid and the daughter's birth centroid, available for every pair
whose two structures were logged (coverage 1.000; structural loss 6.5 % and 35.3 % reported as itself):

| inflow | pairs | rho(distance, daughter area) | rho(mother area, daughter) | partial: area given distance | worlds negative |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 2 024 | **−0.008** | 0.531 | 0.570 | 7/10 |
| 0.040 | 1 139 | **−0.367** | 0.384 | 0.552 | **10/10** |

Registered verdict: **mixed** — the cells disagree. But the criterion for "the topographic reading wins" (the mother's area must
