lose its coefficient once position is known) did not fire in either cell: the area's partial is *higher* than its raw value.
Position adds a term; it does not replace the inherited width.

**X103d** tested the obvious confound of the maze cell — in a labyrinth, distance from a peak is nearly the same coordinate as
half-width of the arm, so any peak might do. The null was the distance to the nearest **non-ancestor** peak at the birth step:

| inflow | rho(distance to mother) | rho(distance to nearest stranger peak) | contrast | worlds negative (mother / stranger) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | −0.008 | **+0.358** | −0.350 | 7/10 / **0/10** |
| 0.040 | **−0.367** | +0.103 | **+0.265** | **10/10** / 2/10 |

Registered verdict for the maze cell: **maternal** — stranger peaks carry the opposite sign, so the term is about the mother. And the
cell that was meant to be an internal null produced the day's cleanest new effect: in the lattice, the farther a daughter is born
from the nearest **other** peak, the larger she grows (+0.358, ten worlds out of ten). That is a crowding term, with its own sign and
its own perfect agreement.

Three separable sources of size, then, measured on the same pairs:

| term | lattice | maze | what it is |
|---|---|---|---|
| mother's excursion area | 0.531 | 0.384 | the inherited width of the excursion (X100–X102) |
| distance to the mother's peak | −0.008 | −0.367 | maternal decay: farther from her maximum, smaller |
| distance to the nearest stranger peak | +0.358 | +0.103 | crowding: farther from a competitor, larger |

This sharpens X100–X102 rather than contradicting them: what is *inherited* is the excursion set; where a daughter sits decides how
much of that inheritance she expresses. It also gives the line its first quantity that is not about ancestry at all — crowding.

Cost of the detour: two voids and two analyses of stored artifacts, no GPU. What it bought: two rules in `docs/design-checks.md`
(coverage denominators name their pairs; a collapse needs a degeneracy table) and the selection fact that every maze number in this
line describes **long-lived** structures only — the reason X105 exists.

Reports: `reports/2026-11-02-x103-basin-position-void.md` (the two voids), `reports/2026-11-02-x103cd-birth-distance.md` (the
answer); aggregates `l1_field_x103_basin_position.json`, `l1_field_x103b_basin_position.json`,
`l1_field_x103c_birth_distance.json`, `l1_field_x103d_peak_null.json`.

## 10fx. Room is a property of the place, and heredity is not made of it (X106–X107, 02.11)

X103d's internal null produced a term nobody had asked for: in the lattice, a structure born farther from the nearest **non-ancestor**
peak grows larger. X106 asked what that coordinate is, and X107 asked the one question that could have reduced every heredity number
in this line — how much of the parent–daughter correlation is the daughter simply sitting in her mother's room.

| inflow | ρ(room, daughter area) | ρ(room, **mother's** area) | ρ(room, lifetime) | heredity | partial given room | partial given both rooms | drop |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | +0.322 | **+0.397** | +0.171 (9/10 worlds) | 0.519 | **0.455** | 0.425 | **0.064** |
| 0.040 | +0.114 | −0.038 | +0.056 | 0.395 | 0.395 | 0.392 | 0.000 |

**X106** registered three readings — a property of places, a constraint on growth, a constraint on persistence — and returned
*mixed*, for a reason that is itself the finding: in the lattice the effect is present for mothers and daughters alike (0.397 against
0.322), so the coordinate describes where a structure sits in the packing rather than anything it inherits; in the maze it is absent
for both (0.114 and −0.038). A labyrinth sets the width of a structure by the geometry of its arms, so "the distance to the next peak"
stops being informative — the nearest peak is often one wall away in the next arm. Lifetime follows weakly in the lattice (+0.171, nine
worlds of ten, below the coarse-quantity floor of 0.20 that the plan set in advance), so the primary reading is **growth in space**,
not duration.

**X107** then removed the room: the coefficient falls from 0.519 to 0.455 in the lattice and from 0.395 to nothing in the maze, and
controlling both rooms (the mother's and the daughter's) takes the lattice value to 0.425. Registered verdict: **heredity is not a
shared-room effect** — the drop is inside the declared resolution of 0.10.

The honest form of that sentence: the room cannot account for the heredity, not that it accounts for nothing. About 12 % of the
lattice coefficient is room, so 0.455 / 0.395 are the room-adjusted values, and any future claim that needs three decimals should
quote them. This is the answer to the referee question the whole line invites — *isn't this just daughters staying near their
mothers?* — and the answer is a number, 0.064.

The two analyses also give the line its second non-ancestral quantity, with a stated domain: room matters where structures compete for
open space and vanishes where the medium already fixes their width. That is a checkable morphological statement, not a statistical
one: an observer can see the arms of the maze and predict where the coordinate should stop working.

Reports: `reports/2026-11-02-x106-crowding.md`, `reports/2026-11-02-x107-shared-room.md`; aggregates
`l1_field_x106_crowding.json`, `l1_field_x107_shared_room.json`. X105 (dense logging, twenty runs) is the replication that removes the
maze cell's long-lived selection.

## 10fy. Dense logging: the maternal decay is real, and the maze heredity was underestimated (X105, 02.11)

Every maze number in this line rested on structures that lived long enough to be logged twice. The arithmetic: at `F = 0.040` the
median lifetime is 89 steps, the logging interval was 50, and **35.3 %** of ancestor–daughter pairs had a member that never appeared in
the component log. X105 reran both cells at `--sample-every 10`, twenty new runs (seeds 301–310), everything else as X100. It is a
replication at a new density, not a refinement of the same worlds: logging density draws the trait mutations, so the seeds make
different worlds (the X85 rule).

| inflow | structural loss | pairs | ρ(distance, daughter) | worlds negative | crowding ρ | worlds positive | heredity ρ(mother, daughter) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 0.065 → **0.021** | 2 211 | −0.041 | 8/10 | **+0.367** | **10/10** | 0.531 → **0.548** |
| 0.040 | 0.353 → **0.088** | 1 951 | **−0.329** | **10/10** | +0.182 | 9/10 | 0.384 → **0.480** |

Both registered verdicts fired: **the maternal decay is robust** (−0.329 against −0.367, ten worlds out of ten, per-world −0.185 …
−0.441) and **crowding is robust** (+0.367 against +0.358, ten out of ten). Neither non-ancestral term was an artefact of the visible
subset, and the lattice sign control held (−0.041, inside ±0.15).

The number that moved is the one nobody was auditing: **the maze heredity was being underestimated.** `ρ(mother, daughter)` at 0.040
is 0.480, not 0.384 — a quarter larger — because the pairs that made it into the sparse sample are the long-lived ones, and in the maze
lifetime and area are inversely related (X92), so the visible pairs were a compressed subset. The lattice cell barely moved.

Consequences. The *levels* of the X101 ladder and of X102's specificity at 0.040 are probably understated by a similar factor; their
*ordering* is a property of definitions applied to the same pairs and should survive. Re-running both on the dense series is analysis
only (X108). Dense logging should be the default for this line: it costs the same ~105 s per run, because the integration dominates.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x105-dense-logging.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x105_dense.json`; series index
`morphogenesis/experiments/L1_field/x105_series_index.json` (20 runs, all ok).

## 10fz. The ladder and the specificity get sharper, not weaker, on dense fields (X108, 02.11)

X101's ladder and X102's specificity were measured on fields saved every 50 steps — one peak in five had a field at its own peak step,
and basin coverage was 95 % / 80 %. X105 then showed the sparse series understates the maze heredity by a quarter. X108 re-measured
both on **the same twenty worlds** with a field at every logged step: saving a field draws no randomness, and seed 301's `series` and
`uid_pairs` are bit-identical to X105's, so this is a re-measurement, not a new sample.

| definition | 0.030 sparse → dense | 0.040 sparse → dense | delta 0.030 | delta 0.040 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| excursion set, `v > 0.20` | 0.531 → **0.548** | 0.384 → **0.480** | — | — |
| basin by steepest ascent | 0.300 → **0.335** | 0.174 → **0.110** | −0.231 → **−0.213** | −0.209 → **−0.370** |

Basin coverage 0.979 / 0.937, partitions 10/10 in both cells, and the excursion-set coefficient reproduces X105's 0.548 / 0.480 to
three decimals — both registered controls fired. Registered verdict: **the ladder stands on dense fields**, and at the maze inflow it
nearly doubles: with every peak measurable, the topological basin stops tracking what the excursion set sees (0.480 against 0.110).

The specificity follows the same pattern:

| inflow | threshold parent / stranger / gap | basin parent / stranger / gap | change |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 0.548 / 0.246 / **0.302** | 0.334 / 0.245 / **0.089** | **−0.213** |
| 0.040 | 0.480 / 0.037 / **0.443** | 0.106 / 0.043 / **0.064** | **−0.379** |

The pre-registered criterion (a fall of ≥ 0.15 in at least one cell) is met twice over — −0.213 and −0.379, the latter larger than the
sparse series' −0.225 — so *specificity is definition-bound too* survives dense logging and gets stronger. The plan's stricter branch
(basin gap ≤ 0.05 in both cells, i.e. no specificity left at all) did not fire: the basin gaps are 0.089 and 0.064. Under the basin
definition a mother still beats a neighbour slightly; under the excursion set she beats it by 0.30–0.44.

What changes in the numbers is the maze cell's threshold-level specificity: 0.443 (parent 0.480, stranger 0.037) against 0.215 sparse.
At dense fields the maze cell is the most lineage-specific cell in the line, where the sparse series had flattened it. The maze is
therefore **more** lineage-specific and **less** basin-specific than the sparse sample suggested — both pointing the same way: the
inherited object is an excursion set, and the field's topography is the stage, not the message.

Both aggregate versions stay in git: `l1_field_x101_watershed.json` / `l1_field_x102_specificity_ladder.json` are the registered
sparse originals, `l1_field_x101b_dense.json` / `l1_field_x102b_dense.json` the corrections. A claim quoting 0.300 / 0.174 at the basin
definition is quoting a sparse-series number.

Reports: `reports/2026-11-02-x108-ladder-on-dense.md`.

## 10ga. A second diffusion length moves the orientation, not the wavelength (X96–X96c, 02.11)

The owner approved the one channel the line had never tried (02.11 18:11, `docs/decision-log.md`): a third field `w`, a slowly
diffusing inhibitor with its own length, entering the autocatalysis as `u·v²/(1 + γ·w)` with `dw/dt = Dw∇²w + β_w(v − w)`. The flag is
off by default and the anchor is the strictest kind: with it off, seed 291 reproduces the committed `x100_f030_s291.json`
**bit-for-bit**.

**The first coupling was a regime change, not a perturbation.** At `γ` 0.5–1.5 the field dies: the control has 69 components and mean
amplitude 0.128, every `w`-on run ends with 1 or 0 components and mean 0.002. The arithmetic is decisive — inside a structure
`w ≈ v ≈ 1`, so `γ = 0.5` cuts the autocatalysis by a third, and the Gray–Scott band does not absorb that. The identical
`max|Δv| = 0.4325` across six settings was the control's own peak: the manipulated fields were empty. Recorded as a void, with the
diagnostic written into `docs/design-checks.md`.

**The mild sweep answers the question.** At `γ` 0.05–0.10, six living cells (15–59 components):

| `Dw` | γ = 0.05 | γ = 0.10 |
|---|---|---|
| 0.08 | q' 8, s 0.704, 47 comps | q' 9, s 0.832, 43 |
| 0.16 | q' 8, s 0.649, 59 | q' 8, s 0.715, 43 |
| 0.32 | q' 8, s 0.636, 39 | q' 8, s 0.837, 15 |

`q'` is 8 or 9 in all six — inside the 5–9 band every forced world has shown — while the symmetry `s` spans **0.636–0.837**. So the
second diffusion length changes *which way the pattern runs*, not how far apart its stripes are. At `γ = 0.20` the field collapses at
two diffusion lengths (4 and 1 components) and at the third it survives with 18 components but coarsens to `q' = 4`, one unit below the
band: reported, not claimed, because the registered criterion was two units.

The reading for the line: three channels for scale are now closed by measurement (inflow, initial condition, second diffusion length),
and the fourth (spatial inflow) sets orientation. The second species is the informative one because it is the knob that *should* set a
wavelength — in a Turing system the ratio of diffusion lengths does exactly that. Here that ratio moves the orientation instead, which
suggests the scale is pinned by the reaction band `(F, k)` and the grid rather than by diffusion geometry. That is a hypothesis.

Caveats: one seed, one cell, nine settings, no per-world statistics; `s` and `q'` are single numbers per setting; `γ = 0.05–0.10` means
a 5–9 per cent cut in the autocatalysis inside structures. X96d is registered to replicate the orientation result on three seeds and to
push `Dw` to 0.64.

Reports: `reports/2026-11-02-x96-void-regime.md`, `reports/2026-11-02-x96bcd-second-species.md`; aggregates
`l1_field_x96_pilot.json`, `l1_field_x96b_pilot.json`, `l1_field_x96c_pilot.json`.

## 10gb. Both room results survive dense logging, and crowding keeps its domain (X109, 02.11)

X107's answer to the referee question — heredity is not a shared-room effect, the room costs 0.064 of 0.519 — had only been measured on
the sample X105 showed to be compressed. X108's twenty runs are the same worlds with a field at every logged step, so the two room
readouts were re-measured there.

| inflow | room coefficient (mother) | control ρ(mother, daughter) | partial given room | both rooms | drop |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | +0.389 | 0.538 | 0.469 | 0.434 | **0.069** |
| 0.040 | −0.025 | 0.487 | 0.487 | 0.480 | **0.000** |

Both controls fired: the heredity coefficient reproduces X108's 0.548 / 0.480 within 0.01, and the room coordinate keeps X106's sign and
size. Registered verdict: **the room result survives** — 0.069 and 0.000 against 0.064 and 0.000 on the sparse series. About 13 % of the
lattice coefficient is room on both samples, and the room-adjusted heredity values on the better sample are **0.469 / 0.487**.

The crowding coefficient replicates too (+0.360 in the lattice against +0.322 sparse and +0.358 in X103d), and it stays a property of
places: it predicts the mother's own area at least as well (+0.394 against +0.360) and does nothing in the maze (−0.016). The one thing
that moved is the lifetime component: +0.171 with 9/10 worlds became **+0.091 with 5/10**, which is what a coin looks like. X106's
"growth rather than persistence" reading therefore gets stronger on the better sample, not weaker.

Caveat that has to travel with the numbers: `l1_field_x106_crowding.json` was rewritten on the dense prefix, so the sparse values
(+0.322 / −0.038, lifetime +0.171) live in git history and in X106's report while the aggregate now holds the dense ones. Any quotation
must name its sample.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x109-room-dense.md`; aggregates `l1_field_x109_room_dense.json`, `l1_field_x106_crowding.json`.

## 10gc. The second species moves neither scale nor orientation, and it shows where each cell sits relative to the edge (X96d, 02.11)

Thirty runs (three seeds × two inflows × `Dw` 0.08–0.64 × a control per seed) at `γ = 0.05`, the coupling X96c's pilot showed
harmless in the lattice.

Registered verdict: **the orientation effect does not replicate** — only two of six cells reach `|ρ(Dw, s)| ≥ 0.8` (the criterion was
four of six with one sign), and those two disagree: −0.4 and +0.8 in the lattice, +1.0 in the maze on three live diffusion lengths.
X96c's spread of 0.636–0.837 was a one-seed property, exactly as its plan warned.

| cell | control q' | live Dw of 4 | q' at Dw = 0.64 | rho(Dw, s) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 seed 411 | 9 | 4 | 8 | −0.4 |
| 0.030 seed 412 | 9 | 4 | 8 | +0.8 |
| 0.030 seed 413 | 9 | 4 | 8 | +0.4 |
| 0.040 seed 411 | 7 | 3 | 7 | +1.0 |
| 0.040 seed 412 | 7 | 2 | 7 | — |
| 0.040 seed 413 | 7 | 1 | 8 | — |

Three facts. **No scale move**: `q'` stays at 7–9 everywhere with `Dw` pushed to eight times `Dv`; the largest change is one unit and the
criterion was two. **The maze cell is fragile**: at a 5 % cut in the autocatalysis the lattice keeps all four diffusion lengths live in
all three seeds while the maze keeps three, two and one. **Orientation is not a channel either**: with three seeds the spread is noise.

This closes the last untried channel for scale with a measured negative. Four negatives now stand behind the line's statement about
scale — inflow (X84, X99), initial condition (X96b), second diffusion length (this series) — and a spatially varying inflow sets
orientation rather than wavelength (X52). **Wavelength is pinned by the reaction band `(F, k)` and the grid**, the remaining candidate.

The fragility is a condition-of-life statement in its own right: the same perturbation a lattice cell absorbs takes the percolating cell
apart, so "how close to the edge a medium sits" is measurable as `γ_cell`, the coupling at which a cell stops being a pattern at all.
In this parameterisation the maze's threshold lies between 0.05 and 0.10, and the lattice's above 0.20 (X96b's first pass survived
nothing at 0.50, so the gap is at least fourfold). X110 is registered to measure it by bisection.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x96d-orientation.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x96d_orientation.json`; series index
`morphogenesis/experiments/L1_field/x96d_series_index.json` (30 runs, all ok).

## 10gd. The area–lifetime relation sits at the null's edge, and the logging density does not move it (X111–X111b, 02.11)

X92's "size is not a currency" rested on coefficients of +0.191 / +0.067 / −0.041 / −0.046 across four cells, measured on lifetimes
quantised by a 50-step logging interval. X105 then showed that interval was hiding a third of the maze cell's genealogies, and its dense
series measures lifetime in 10-step units. X111 asked whether the sign survives the finer measurement; it voided on its own control,
because the plan took X92's 0.025/0.035 values for the 0.030/0.040 cells (CLAIMS 312 lists them per cell). Recomputing on X92's **own**
series reproduces it to within 0.004 (+0.069 against +0.067, −0.042 against −0.046), so the code is right and the reference was
mis-assigned; X111b re-registered with the reference measured on the right worlds and with the comparison stated as two samples of one
design at two densities, never one sample twice.

| cell | X92 (own worlds) | recomputed | x100 sparse | x108 dense | null p95 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | +0.067 | +0.069 | +0.077 | +0.049 | 0.042 / 0.041 |
| 0.040 | −0.046 | −0.042 | +0.021 | −0.039 | 0.047 / 0.041 |

Registered verdict: **the sign is set by the sample, not the density** — the deltas are −0.028 and −0.060, inside the 0.10 resolution,
and every coefficient in the x100/x108 comparison sits inside its own shuffle null.

What follows for the claim: X92's headline survives, but its support is weaker than the numbers looked. The live coefficient is about
0.07 (6–8 % of variance) at best and on two of four samples here it does not clear a 0.04 null. The honest formulation is "no consequence
of size has been demonstrated above the noise floor of this estimator", not "size does not pay". A sibling design meant to detect a
0.05 effect (X93) would need samples where the null is below 0.02 — roughly four times the structures the current series carry.

Caveats: only the two cells the dense series covers; 1 000 within-cell shuffles; lifetimes are `last − first + 1` on logged steps.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x111-area-lifetime.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x111b_area_lifetime.json`.

## 10ge. gamma_cell = 0.30 in both cells: a boundary that does not distinguish the habitats (X110–X110b, 02.11)

X96d's by-product — the maze losing its diffusion lengths at gamma = 0.05 while the lattice kept them — looked like a statement about how
close each cell sits to the edge of existence. X110 measured the coupling ceiling directly: bisect gamma at Dw = 0.16, three seeds per
cell.

X110's own liveness rule (ten components) was wrong and produced a counter-example immediately: at gamma = 0.50 seed 423 the lattice ends
with sixteen **23-cell specks**, occupied 0.04 — a shattered field that a count calls alive. X110b was registered while the lattice column
was complete and the maze column did not exist: alive iff occupied >= 0.05 **and** the largest component >= 50 cells, with two alternative
threshold sets printed beside the answer.

| cell | gamma = 0.10 | gamma = 0.30 | gamma = 0.50 | gamma_cell |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 49 structures, 25 % occupied | 13 / 18 / 1 structures, 6-13 % | dead (0.004, 37 cells) | **0.30** |
| 0.040 | one body of 1 333-3 030 cells, 41 % | two bodies of 596-640 cells, 7-10 % | dead (0.008, 37 cells) | **0.30** |

Registered verdict: **the cells do not differ** — gamma_cell is 0.30 under 0.05/50, 0.04/40 and 0.06/60 in both. So `gamma_cell = 0.30`
means both cells survive a **23 %** cut in the autocatalysis inside a structure (1/1.3 = 0.77) and die by 33 % (1/1.5 = 0.67). That is the
first quantitative boundary for this world, and it is the same in both habitats — a negative result about "distance to the edge" as a
property that separates them.

**Correction to §10gc, appended there.** The fragility X96d found belongs to `Dw`, not to `gamma`: at `gamma = 0.05` the maze lost its
diffusion lengths because they were long (0.32, 0.64), and at a fixed short one both cells share the same ceiling. The correct statement is
narrower: the maze cell tolerates a **long-range** inhibitor less.

What does differ is morphology under coupling: at gamma 0.10-0.30 the maze is a single percolating body while the lattice is 1-49 separate
structures. Both pass the amended liveness test by design, and every table prints the largest component so the difference is visible.

Caveats: one box, one grid, one k, one Dw for the sweep; gamma_cell is a bracket on a five-point grid, and a finer grid around 0.30-0.50
(twelve runs) would turn it into a number.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x110-gamma-cell.md`; aggregates `l1_field_x110b_gamma_cell.json`, `l1_field_x110_gamma_cell.json`.

## 10gf. The cut moves the level a little and the specificity not at all (X112b, 02.11)

X112 tried to get a cut sweep for free by re-measuring areas off X108's saved fields and voided: identity is the logger's labeling of the
field at the cut under test, and no anchoring scheme reproduced the 0.20 numbers (0.771, 0.521, 0.491, 0.410 against 0.548). X112b does it
the way X99 did — new runs at each threshold — and it is a **paired** design, because `--threshold` is an observer parameter: the ten seeds
at 0.15 and 0.25 are the same worlds X108 measured at 0.20.

| cut | pairs | rho1 | delta vs 0.20 | specificity gap | delta | median area |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.15 | 2 262 | **0.554** | **+0.006** | 0.284 | −0.018 | 118 |
| 0.20 (X108) | 2 211 | **0.548** | — | 0.302 | — | 83 |
| 0.25 | 3 161 | **0.445** | **−0.103** | 0.326 | +0.024 | 49 |

Registered verdict: **the level is cut-bound; the gap is cut-stable** — the level moves one step past the declared 0.10 at the harshest cut
and nowhere else, while the gap does not move inside 0.10 at either.

The asymmetry is the result. Changing the *kind* of definition (excursion set → drainage basin, X102) collapses the gap from 0.312 / 0.215
to 0.017 / −0.010; changing the *cut* of the same definition leaves it at 0.284 / 0.302 / 0.326. So specificity is robust to **where** the
level sits and not robust to **whether the definition is an excursion at all** — a cleaner statement than either result alone.

Second, smaller: **a higher cut produces more pairs, not fewer** (3 161 at 0.25 against 2 262 at 0.15) — cutting higher fragments structures,
and fragments divide more often than they vanish, with median area falling 118 → 49 cells. Worth remembering whenever a cut is chosen for
convenience.

The direction X112's failed schemes suggested (higher at 0.15, lower at 0.25) was half right: the 0.25 part held, the 0.15 part did not.

Caveats: one cell, ten worlds per cut, SE 0.019–0.023; the −0.103 is just past the bar; the stranger control comes from the same frame, so a
higher cut moves both sides of the comparison at once.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x112b-cuts.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x112b_cuts.json`; series index
`morphogenesis/experiments/L1_field/x112b_series_index.json` (20 runs, all ok).

## 10gg. The maze cell is cut-stable too, and the order between cells does not reverse (X112c, 02.11)

X112b gave the lattice cell a clean separation: the level moves one step with the cut (0.554 / 0.548 / 0.445) and the specificity gap does not
(0.284 / 0.302 / 0.326). X99 had found the **half-life's** inflow ordering reversing at 0.25, so the maze cell was the open question.

Twenty runs at `F = 0.040`, seeds 301-310, thresholds 0.15 and 0.25 — the same worlds X108 measured at 0.20, because the threshold is an
observer parameter.

| cut | pairs (used) | rho1 | delta vs 0.20 | gap | delta | median area |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.15 | 882 (460) | **0.408** | −0.072 | 0.322 | −0.121 | 1 775 |
| 0.20 (X108) | 1 951 | **0.480** | — | **0.443** | — | 321 |
| 0.25 | 3 003 (2 356) | **0.420** | −0.060 | 0.347 | −0.096 | 93 |

Registered verdict: **both cut-stable** — level deltas inside 0.10, gap deltas inside 0.15. **No cross-cell reversal**: at 0.25 the lattice is
0.445 and the maze 0.420, a gap of 0.025 against an 0.08 bar. X99's reversal belongs to the half-life, not to these readouts.

Together with X112b the statement is complete for both cells:

| cell | level 0.15 / 0.20 / 0.25 | gap 0.15 / 0.20 / 0.25 |
|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 0.554 / 0.548 / 0.445 | 0.284 / 0.302 / 0.326 |
| 0.040 | 0.408 / 0.480 / 0.420 | 0.322 / 0.443 / 0.347 |

The definition-dependence of X101/X102 is therefore **not** a cut artefact: changing the cut moves the level by about a tenth and the gap by
less, while changing the definition kind moves the level by 0.2-0.37 and the gap by 0.21-0.38. The line's central claim holds at three cuts in
both cells.

Two defects were caught before the numbers: a copied runner kept its old cell tag (seven mislabeled artifacts deleted, tag now derived from
FLOW with an assertion) and the liveness floor was a component count, which rejects the maze's one percolating body by construction — the
amendment uses X110b's structural rule and discloses that two of twenty runs had been summarised when it was written.

Caveats: coverage at 0.15 is 460 of 882 pairs (the maze is one body of 1 775 cells there, so many uids share a component); ten worlds per cut;
the 0.20 reference is X108's own number.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x112c-cuts-maze.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x112c_cuts_maze.json`; series index (20 runs, all ok).

## 10gh. The sister difference is inherited into the second generation, and it grows there (X97, 02.11)

X93 measured sisters in one generation; whether the difference belongs to a **lineage** or to a single structure is decided one generation
later. X108's dense logs carry `--split-new-uid`, so the three-level genealogy is readable without new runs.

| cell | sister pairs | heredity (control) | first generation: larger - smaller | second generation: granddaughters | bootstrap 95 % CI | z |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 282 | 0.548 | +4.94 cells (z 2.43) | **+12.90** | **[10.11, 15.80]** | 8.84 |
| 0.040 | 186 | 0.480 | +88.06 cells (z 2.12) | **+170.91** | **[127.9, 228.9]** | 6.69 |

Registered verdict: **the advantage persists** — both intervals exclude zero, and the difference is larger in the second generation than in the
first (+12.9 against +4.9 in the lattice, and almost double in the maze). It is the line's first two-generation statement.

The reading is deliberately narrow. This is **exactly what size heredity predicts** (ρ₁ ≈ 0.5 means a larger sister should have larger
daughters and granddaughters), so the result is a heredity statement, **not** evidence that size pays — X92/X111 put the consequence side at
the noise floor. What the numbers add beyond the expectation is the direction of change: the advantage **grows** rather than decays, which a
0.5 coefficient does not require.

Two untested mechanisms can produce that growth: branching arithmetic (a larger structure has more cells that can divide, so its line
branches more and the mean is pulled up by the number of branches) and a genuine lineage effect. The separating test is registered: compare
the **median** granddaughter with the mean and count grandchildren per branch.

Caveats: the granddaughter filter leaves 282 and 186 sister pairs; two cells only; the first-generation readout here is area difference, not
X93's lifetime share, so the two are not interchangeable; areas are threshold-0.20 excursion areas, which X112b puts at about a tenth of cut
correction.

Report: `reports/2026-11-02-x97-sisters-second-generation.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x97_sisters_second_generation.json`.

## 10gi. The second-generation sister advantage is a lineage effect, not branching arithmetic (X97b, 03.11)

X97's mean difference (+12.90 cells) could have been carried by **how many** grandchildren a branch produces: a larger structure has more cells
that can divide. The median is insensitive to that, and the branch counts measure it directly.

| cell | pairs | mean | median | median / mean | grandchildren per branch |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 286 | 12.93 | **12.68** | **98 %** | 2.024 / 2.000 (+1.2 %) |
| 0.040 | 205 | 171.74 | **145.96** | **85 %** | 2.093 / 2.005 (+4.4 %) |

Registered verdict: **a lineage effect is needed** — the median holds at 98 % and 85 % of the mean while the branch counts differ by 1-4 %,
against a 10 % bar. The larger sister's granddaughters are individually larger, not merely more numerous. Controls: heredity 0.548 / 0.480 and
the mean reproducing X97 within 0.5 %.

This closes the doubt that X97 was an artefact of branch counting. It does **not** close the consequence question: a lineage whose structures
are larger is not a lineage that does better — X92 and X111 put any consequence of size at the noise floor, and X93's lifetime advantage
reverses in the maze.

So the day's honest statement: **size is a lineage property in this medium** — inherited (ρ₁ ≈ 0.5), surviving a generation as a difference
that grows (X97), not an arithmetic consequence of branching (X97b) — **but nothing yet shows that inheriting it buys anything.** That is the
shape of the answer to the owner's question: a medium can transmit a trait faithfully and offer no advantage for it.

Caveats: the median of a two-grandchild branch is coarse (98 % agreement with the mean is partly luck of the distribution); 286 and 205 pairs,
the maze count slightly above X97's 186 because the peak filter differs; two cells, one seed set, one box.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x97b-sisters-median.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x97b_sisters_median.json`.

## 10gj. Size buys generations — the line's first measured consequence (X113, 03.11)

Every earlier attempt at "consequence" used lifetime (X92, X93), which is entangled with size and sits at the noise floor. This one uses
**lineage depth**: how many generations a line reaches. Not a size, not a lifetime.

| cell | uids with descendants | rho(area, depth) | partial given lifetime | rho(depth, lifetime) | heredity | sister contrast | 95 % CI | pairs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 1 073 | **0.467** | **0.459** | −0.185 | 0.548 | **+1.479 generations** | **[1.289, 1.654]** | 1 142 |
| 0.040 | 832 | **0.332** | **0.340** | −0.070 | 0.480 | **+2.213 generations** | **[1.962, 2.489]** | 1 195 |

Registered verdict: **size buys generations.** The sister contrast excludes zero in both cells and the partial of area given lifetime is
0.459 / 0.340. Both controls fired: heredity 0.548 / 0.480, and depth is not lifetime in disguise — they correlate negatively (−0.185,
−0.070), so the readout does different work from X92's.

It is the line's first consequence: a lineage whose structures are larger reaches more generations, on 1 142-1 195 sister pairs, in both cells.
It is **not yet** evidence that the larger line is faster or fitter: depth counts generations, and a bigger structure has more cells that can
divide, so it may reach more generations in the same time by arithmetic rather than advantage — the same worry X97b answered for the
granddaughters' sizes. The separating number is a **rate**, generations per unit lifetime, and the negative depth-lifetime correlation hints
it will be stronger. Registered as X113b.

Caveats: horizon truncation (median depth 3 of a 6 000-step run, so lines alive at the end are favoured); leaves are excluded from the
correlation and the denominator is stated; the cells differ in magnitude (1.5 against 2.2), and the maze is where X93's lifetime advantage
reverses, so their agreement in sign here is information rather than a given.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x113-depth.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x113_depth.json`.

## 10gk. The generation advantage is speed, not arithmetic (X113b–X113c, 03.11)

X113 measured the larger sister's line reaching +1.479 / +2.213 more generations, with depth negatively correlated with lifetime. Two mechanisms
could produce that count: faster propagation, or more division events per cell. A rate separates them.

X113b voided on its own control: a rate needs `life >= 50` steps, so the sample is filtered (83 of 1 087 uids excluded in the lattice, 369 of 877 in
the maze), and the plan required the filtered contrast to reproduce the unfiltered number within 5 %. It came back 8.6 % and 10 % lower — the
borrowed-control-band error, committed by the plan. X113c measures the filter's effect directly, by computing the depth contrast on both samples in
the same run and allowing a 15 % shift.

| cell | usable uids (excluded) | generations per 1 000 steps | 95 % CI | rho(area, rate) | per-division contrast | 95 % CI |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 1 004 (83) | **+6.06** | **[5.03, 7.08]** | **0.414** | **+0.162** | [0.146, 0.180] |
| 0.040 | 508 (369) | **+18.96** | **[14.89, 23.30]** | 0.115 | **+0.124** | [0.105, 0.144] |

Registered verdict: **size buys speed.** Both cells gain generations per unit time with intervals far from zero, `rho(area, rate)` clears the 0.10
bar, and the per-division contrast also excludes zero — so the advantage is not the number of divisions alone: per child, the larger sister's line
still goes deeper.

The chain the line has been closing all day is closed **in one currency**: size is inherited (0.548 / 0.480), it spans a generation as a size
difference that grows (X97/X97b), and it pays in generations at a faster rate (X113/X113c). What stays open is whether that payment matters for
persistence — generations are propagation, not survival, and the maze is where the lifetime advantage reverses.

Caveats: the maze filter drops 42 % of uids; a rate is a ratio of two noisy quantities and the bootstrap is on paired differences only; depth is
truncated by the 6 000-step horizon (median 3); one seed set, one box.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x113bcd-depth-rate.md`; aggregates `l1_field_x113c_depth_rate.json` (and X113's `l1_field_x113_depth.json`).

## 10gl. The generation advantage survives an equal observation window (X114, 03.11)

X113 and X113c measured depth over the whole run, so a line that starts late or dies early has less time to go deep and lines alive at the horizon are
favoured. X114 gives both branches of a sister pair the same budget: generations reached inside `W = 3 000` steps of each branch's own first appearance,
counting only descendants born inside that window.

| cell | pairs | heredity | median depth windowed vs whole-run | rate contrast per 1 000 windowed steps | 95 % CI |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 1 142 | 0.548 | **2 vs 3** | **+0.407** | **[0.362, 0.453]** |
| 0.040 | 1 195 | 0.480 | **2 vs 3** | **+0.638** | **[0.561, 0.720]** |

Registered verdict: **the advantage survives** — both intervals exclude zero, and the window demonstrably bites (median depth 3 → 2 in both cells), so it
is not the previous measurement in disguise. The magnitudes are not comparable with X113c's +6.06 / +18.96: that rate divides by each branch's own
lifetime, this one by the fixed window. Two denominators, one question, the same answer.

So the truncation caveat carried by the day's consequence claim is tested and does not explain the effect. What stays open: whether reaching generations
is worth anything for persistence, and whether `W = 3 000` is the right horizon — a design choice, registered so it can be attacked.

**Data restored.** The same disk cleanup that removed nineteen tracked files also deleted the full X108 logs (50 MB), which had never been compacted or
committed, so this analysis could not run. The series was re-run from its seeds and reproduces the committed aggregates **exactly** — `x113`'s
0.467 / 0.332, partial 0.459 / 0.340, depth–lifetime −0.185 / −0.070, sister contrasts +1.479 and +2.213 with the same intervals, and the excursion-set
heredity 0.548 / 0.480. The reconstruction is a repair because the world is deterministic, and the `.compact.json` projections of all twenty runs are now
committed (5.9 MB) so the loss cannot recur.

Caveats: `W` is half the run; about 450 pairs per cell have a window that runs past the series end (nominal rather than exact); the two rate denominators
must not be compared in size.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x114-window.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x114_window.json`.

## 10gm. Faster lines persist: the consequence acquires a survival currency (X115, 03.11)

X113–X114 gave the larger sister's lineage more generations, faster, on equal windows. The currency that matters for "conditions for life" is whether the
line still exists at the end. Paired within sister pairs, on X108's restored logs:

| cell | pairs | heredity | survival difference (larger - smaller) | 95 % CI | only larger / only smaller survive | both / neither |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 1 142 | 0.548 | **+0.250** | **[0.220, 0.279]** | **324 / 39** | 641 / 138 |
| 0.040 | 1 195 | 0.480 | **+0.137** | **[0.116, 0.159]** | **178 / 14** | 40 / 963 |

Registered verdict: **faster lines persist better** — both intervals far from zero, same direction in both cells, and among the discriminating pairs the
asymmetry is 8:1 in the lattice and 13:1 in the maze. The secondary readout (living descendants at the end) agrees: +0.349 (CI 0.319-0.380) and +0.141
(CI 0.120-0.163). Controls: heredity 0.548 / 0.480 and the depth contrast reproducing X113 within 5 %, i.e. the same pairs measured for a new question.

The day's arc now reads:

| claim | measurement | cells |
|---|---|---|
| size is inherited | rho1 0.548 / 0.480; room-corrected 0.469 / 0.487 | both |
| it is a lineage property across a generation | granddaughters +12.68 cells (median, 98 % of the mean), branch counts within 4 % | both |
| it buys generations, faster | +1.479 / +2.213 generations; +6.06 / +18.96 per 1 000 steps; survives equal windows | both |
| **and it buys survival** | **+0.250 / +0.137 end-survival, 324:39 and 178:14** | both |

So the hedge is retired: a medium that transmits a size faithfully also pays for it, in generations and in persistence. X92's "size is not a currency"
remains true in its own terms — *lifetime* is not the currency and the maze reverses it — while survival of the line is a different quantity, and there
size pays in both cells.

Caveats: the maze barely survives at all (963 of 1 195 pairs have neither branch alive, so its effect rests on 232 pairs); "alive at the end" is presence
in one snapshot, not resilience; the mechanism is not identified, since a bigger line has more cells and more branches and can lose members and still be
present — X97b ruled that out for one generation, not for survival.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x115-persistence.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x115_persistence.json`.

## 10gn. Half to two-thirds of the survival advantage is branch count (X116, 03.11)

X115 found the larger sister's line alive at the end +0.250 / +0.137 more often. A larger structure has more cells and often more daughters, so it can lose
members and still be present. X116 matches sister pairs on the **number of daughters** and compares survival among matched pairs.

| cell | pairs full → matched | mean daughters difference before → after | full | **matched** | 95 % CI | only larger / smaller (matched) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 1 142 → **633** | 0.825 → **0.000** | 0.250 | **+0.125** | **[0.092, 0.160]** | **104 / 25** |
| 0.040 | 1 195 → **631** | 0.894 → **0.000** | 0.137 | **+0.044** | **[0.022, 0.067]** | **39 / 11** |

Both controls fired: the full-sample contrast reproduces X115 within 5 %, and matching removes the confound by construction. The advantage more than halves
(0.250 → 0.125 in the lattice, 0.137 → 0.044 in the maze), so **branch count explains about half of the lattice advantage and about two-thirds of the maze
advantage** — the arithmetic objection is partly right and X115's headline was partly a count of daughters. It does not explain all of it: the residual is
positive with an interval excluding zero in both cells, above the 0.10 resolution in the lattice and below it in the maze.

Registered verdict: **the advantage is not branch count** (the rule asked whether the matched interval excludes zero, and it does); the report adds what the
rule did not — the residual's size is above resolution in one cell and below it in the other.

So X115's claim is quoted with X116 beside it: the larger sister's line is alive at the end more often, **half to two-thirds of that is having more
daughters**, and the non-arithmetic remainder is strongest where the medium is a lattice.

Caveats: matching discards ~45 % of the pairs; matching on daughters does not match on cells, and the larger sister still has more; survival is presence in
one snapshot; the maze barely survives at all (963 of 1 195 pairs have neither branch alive).

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x116-matched.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x116_matched.json`.

## 10go. X118–X119: matching cannot separate size from daughters, and a model finds a cost of division (03.11)

X118 kept the exposure fixed (sisters within ±10 %, widened to ±15 %) and varied the mediator (different daughter counts). The subset came back at 22 and 17
pairs with area ratios 0.919 / 0.906 — the design is void, and the diagnosis is structural: sisters resemble each other in size (that is the heredity measured
all day) and daughter count rises with size, so the two conditions fight over the same tail. **Size and daughter counts are not separable by matching inside
sister pairs.**

X119 answers the same question with a model instead: pool every uid, fit `alive ~ log(area) + daughters` with a world-level bootstrap.

| cell | uids | alive at end | area coefficient | 95 % CI | daughters coefficient | 95 % CI |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 2 257 | 29.2 % | **+0.363** | [0.258, 0.468] | **−0.429** | **[-0.509, -0.345]** |
| 0.040 | 1 993 | 3.0 % | **+0.052** | [0.043, 0.062] | **−0.051** | **[-0.061, -0.043]** |

Both coefficients exclude zero in both cells and point in opposite directions: **size helps the structure's own survival, and having more daughters hurts it**.
That is a cost of division — the first this line has seen — and it explains why X115's raw survival advantage was so large and why X116 halved it: the two
channels run against each other.

The confound that must be named first: **age**. A structure with many daughters has usually lived longer, and a longer life gives more time either to be
present at the horizon or to die before it. The model controls area, not age, so the honest statement is "at fixed size, more daughters predicts lower
survival; whether that is a cost of division or the age of the structure is not separated here". Age is on the logs, so the control is analysis-only: X120.

This makes the day's consequence picture two-signed rather than one-signed: size → own survival positive, daughters → own survival negative, size → lineage
survival positive on the raw contrast and halving when daughters are matched.

Caveats: the maze's alive rate is 3 %, so its coefficients rest on few survivors; OLS on a binary outcome is a linear probability model; "alive" is the uid's own
presence, not its subtree's; one seed set, one box, the 0.20 cut.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x118-x119-daughters-cost.md`; aggregates `l1_field_x118_mediator.json` (void), `l1_field_x119_model.json`.

## 10gp. Daughters are an early-life property, and the time confound is weaker than assumed (X120, 03.11)

X120 tried to separate "cost of division" from "time to divide" by counting daughters inside a fixed 1 000-step window after birth. Its own control fired
against it: the early count correlates with the whole-life count at **0.868** (lattice) and **0.983** (maze), above the registered 0.8 bar, so the design is
void.

The correlation is the finding: **divisions happen early in a structure's life.** A 1 000-step window captures almost all daughters, so "many daughters" is not
"lived long enough to divide a lot" — it is a property of the first fifth of a life. X119's named confound is therefore much weaker than its report assumed:
structures do not accumulate daughters over a long life; they divide early or not at all.

The model's other coefficients, recorded without a verdict resting on them:

| cell | uids (excluded late births) | alive | area | daughters in 1 000 steps | start time |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 1 891 (366) | 29.3 % | +0.013 (CI −0.060…0.062) | **−0.268 (CI −0.300…−0.215)** | −0.021 (CI −0.041…0.073) |
| 0.040 | 1 976 (17) | 2.3 % | +0.016 (CI 0.009…0.025) | **−0.021 (CI −0.025…−0.017)** | **+0.077 (CI 0.053…0.107)** |

The early daughter count keeps X119's negative sign in both cells, and start time does **not** take a negative sign (uninformative in the lattice, positive in the
maze), so "later-born structures are less likely present" is not what the model finds.

What remains uncontrolled is the **hazard** reading of age — an older structure has had more time to die before the horizon — and X120 cannot address it because
a dead structure's age is its outcome. The claim stays "at fixed size, more daughters predicts lower own-survival; the mechanism is not identified", with the
caveat sharpened: **time-to-divide is largely excluded, hazard is not tested**. A survival model over the intermediate snapshots is registered as X121.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x120-window-daughters-void.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x120_window_daughters.json`.

## 10gq. The cost of division was the identity convention (X122, 03.11)

X121's person-period table could not produce a non-zero daughter count, and the diagnosis retired X119's headline. With `--split-new-uid` a structure's uid ends
**exactly when it divides**: uid 1 is present at steps 1-210 and its children appear at 220; uid 2 at 220-330 with children at 340; uid 5 at 340-490 with children
at 500. So "has daughters" and "present at the end under that uid" are mutually exclusive by construction, and X119's −0.429 (CI −0.509…−0.345) is a restatement
of the identity rule rather than a cost of division. **The claim is withdrawn as an artefact**, not hedged.

Three readouts must now be kept apart, and the line had been sloppy about it:

| readout | meaning | used by |
|---|---|---|
| uid present at the end | undivided and alive | X119, X121 (misread as survival) |
| subtree alive at the end | the lineage continues | X115, X116 (correct for the question) |
| generations reached | depth of the lineage | X113, X114 |

Everything else from the night's consequence work stands: size is inherited; it is a lineage property across a generation (X97/X97b); it buys generations faster
(X113/X114); larger lineages persist more often (X115/X116, halving on branch counts). Only the cost-of-division reading falls, and it falls because the tracker's
identity rule makes it a tautology.

Rule earned: an identity convention is part of every survival readout; a model predicting a uid's presence must not carry a count of its own divisions, and every
lineage statistic states whether it uses the uid or the subtree.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x122-identity-artefact.md`.

## 10gr. A uid's life is one generation: the identity rule, confirmed in code and pinned by a gate (X123, 03.11)

`scripts/field_track.py` gives each surviving component the uid of its best-overlapping predecessor, **unless** it is a split daughter under `--split-new-uid`, in
which case it gets a fresh uid. At a split every daughter whose best overlap is the mother is such a child, so **no daughter inherits the mother's uid** and her
identity ends. The comment beside the rule says why: a daughter needs her own identity or her peak is inseparable from the mother's and size inheritance cannot be
measured. The retirement is a consequence of that choice, and it is now the line's official reading of what a uid means.

Checked on the data, not only in the code: `scripts/check_uid_rule.py` asserts that no parent's last logged appearance comes after its child's first —
**20 runs, 4 162 pairs, zero violations** — and the check is a gate in `make verify` (1.7 s). A rule that lives only in a comment is a rule nobody enforces.

The vocabulary consequence: a uid's lifetime is **time from birth to first division**, i.e. a **generation time**. Every number the line called "lifetime" is one:

| where | measures | correct name |
|---|---|---|
| X88/X89 half-life of size correlations | decay per generation | generation-indexed memory (name stands) |
| X92 area vs lifetime | area vs time to division | area vs **generation time** |
| X93/X97 sisters' lifetimes | sisters' generation times | **generation times** |
| X113/X114 depth and rate | generations per unit time | stands — the same quantity |

The numbers do not change; the words do, and the manuscript must not let "lifetime" mean persistence. X122's withdrawal is now explained by the code as well as by
the data, and X121 is cancelled rather than fixed: a hazard model on uid presence would model "did not divide", which is not a hazard of death.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x123-uid-rule.md`; gate `scripts/check_uid_rule.py`.

## 10gs. The consequence numbers were three statistics wearing one word — audited, re-derived, and the contradiction retired (X124–X125c, 03.11)

Four runs in one night, three of them void, and the net effect is that the block's consequence claims can now be printed with their units. X124 audited the
coherence of the generation-time, depth and rate results, voided by its own control, and named two contradictions of vocabulary. X125 was to fix the definitions
and voided too — its control fired at a factor of three and eight. X125b printed the missing filter and reproduced X113c to three decimals, then voided on a
pair-count floor. X125c closed the last rule — ties. The numbers below are from X108's twenty dense runs, the same pairs every time.

| quantity | definition | filter, printed with the number | published statistic |
|---|---|---|---|
| generation time | uid's last logged step − first + 1, logged steps | none; ties reported | fraction of **non-tied** pairs, with the mean and its tail decomposition |
| depth | longest descendant chain below the uid, generations | none | **mean** paired difference |
| rate | `depth / generation time × 1000`, generations per 1 000 steps | both sisters ≥ 50 steps | **pair-weighted mean**, with its denominator |

| cell | pairs | ties | fraction larger, ties removed | X93 | fraction larger, ties counted | mean difference | rate, filtered (denominator) | rate, unfiltered | depth |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 1 142 | 22.2 % | **0.526** (0.493–0.557) | 0.530 | 0.409 | −530 over non-tied, −412 over all | **6.056** (957 of 1 142) | 36.571 | +1.479 |
| 0.040 | 1 195 | 21.5 % | **0.388** (0.356–0.419) | 0.401 | 0.305 | −36 (CI −79…+7) | **18.96** (638 of 1 195) | 321.48 | +2.213 |

**The rate gap was a filter, not a weighting.** X124's diagnosis — that X113c averaged per uid while X124 averaged per pair — is **withdrawn**: reading
`x113c_analyse.py` back, `rate_diff` is accumulated inside the sister-pair loop, exactly as in X124. X113c also kept a pair only when both generation times were
≥ 50 steps, and that rule alone takes 36.6 to 6.06 and 321.5 to 18.96, to the digit. The filter also qualifies the published number: at `F = 0.040` the rate rests
on **638 of 1 195** pairs, because in the maze half of all sister pairs have a generation time too short to measure a rate on. The lattice keeps 84 %.

**The generation-time contradiction was ties.** X93 skipped pairs whose generation times were equal — 22 % of them — and reported 0.530 / 0.401. X125b counted
ties as "not larger" and reported 0.409 / 0.305. With X93's rule restored on X108's pairs the fractions are **0.526** and **0.388**, and X93's values sit inside
both intervals; the identity `p_all = p_nt × (1 − t)` closes to 1e-13. The two series never disagreed. What the line had done was compare a tie-excluded proportion
with a tie-counted one and call the difference a contradiction.

**And both signs were true, for different questions.** In the lattice the larger sister's generation time is the longer one in a bare majority of non-tied pairs
(0.526, interval lower end 0.493), while in magnitude her generation time is **shorter** — 1 038 against 1 450 steps, 28 % of pairs differing by more than
±1 000 steps, the average positive difference +752 and the negative −1 952. The majority statement is frequent and small; the magnitude statement is a tail. In the
maze both point the same way (0.388, mean −36): the larger sister divides sooner there, and her line still reaches +2.213 generations, because her generation is
short *and* her subtree deep.

**Depth never moved.** Three independent recomputations of the depth contrast give +1.479 and +2.213 to the digit. It is the one consequence number that has
survived every definitional check, and it is what the manuscript may state first: *the larger sister's line reaches more generations, +1.5 in the lattice and +2.2
in the maze, and the claim does not depend on which generation-time statistic is quoted.*

Two claims are withdrawn and one is restricted: the mean of the ratios `g_big/g_small` (12.0 / 13.7) as unusable; "the larger sister lives longer" as unqualified
(it depends on the statistic); and the rate quoted without its filter and denominator. Next hypotheses are the ones this audit opened: whether the maze's 47 % of
short generations is a property of the cell or of the twenty seeds (a `g < 50` census across the ladder), and whether the generation-time excess in the lattice is
the size threshold doing the work — the natural next manipulation being a run whose split rule is area-based.

Reports: `reports/2026-11-03-x124-coherence-void.md`, `-x125-definitions-void.md`, `-x125b-statistics-void.md`, `-x125c-ties.md`; aggregates
`l1_field_x124_coherence.json`, `l1_field_x125_definitions.json`, `l1_field_x125b_statistics.json`, `l1_field_x125c_ties.json`.

## 10gt. The third cell: the clock is a gradient, lineage depth is a threshold, and the best habitat for a lineage is between them (X126, 03.11)

Every consequence number in this line rested on two cells that differ by nearly a factor of two, so the line could not tell a gradient in inflow from a step at
percolation. Ten worlds at `F = 0.035` on X108's own seeds make the comparison three-cell and paired by seed. The anchor is exact — the same flags without field
saving reproduce `x108_f030_s301.json` key for key, `uid_pairs` included — and both known cells re-derive their published numbers to the digit in the same code
path, which is what licenses reading the new cell at all.

| readout | 0.030 | **0.035** | 0.040 | class of the new cell |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| depth, generations | +1.479 | **+1.416** | +2.213 | **step**: inside the lattice's interval, 4.65 SE below the chord |
| rate per 1 000 steps | 6.056 | **9.007** | 18.96 | bend: between the neighbours, 3.19 SE below the chord |
| tie-excluded fraction larger | 0.526 | **0.472** | 0.388 | **gradient**: 0.92 SE from the midpoint |
| below-floor share (`g < 50`) | 0.162 | **0.302** | 0.466 | **gradient**: 0.90 SE from the midpoint |
| end-survival, larger branch | +0.2496 | **+0.2984** | +0.1372 | **reversal**: 5.99 SE above the chord |
| pairs / kept | 1 142 / 957 | 1 106 / 772 | 1 195 / 638 | — |

**The clock runs continuously; depth does not.** Generations shorten smoothly as the food rises (16 % → 30 % → 47 % of pairs below the 50-step floor) and the
larger sister's directional advantage decays smoothly with them (0.526 → 0.472 → 0.388), so the maze is the far end of one axis rather than a different kind of
world. Lineage depth, by contrast, does not move at all between the lattice and the middle cell (+1.479 → +1.416) and then jumps at the percolating one (+2.213,
largest component 935 cells against 441). **Half again as much food buys no deeper lineage; percolation does.** The rate behaves as that pair of statements
requires — flat depth over a shortening generation raises it (6.06 → 9.01, below the chord), and the maze moves both terms at once (18.96).

**And persistence reverses, which is the finding the two-cell design could not have made.** End-survival of the larger branch is +0.2496 → **+0.2984** → +0.1372:
a maximum in the middle cell, 5.99 SE above the chord, with only-the-larger-alive rising 28 % → 37 % → 15 % and both-alive collapsing 56 % → 22 % → 3 %. The
rise from the lattice is **not** resolved (the intervals overlap by 0.014); the fall into the maze is.

So the habitat that maximises generations is not the habitat that maximises a lineage's chance of still being there: depth needs percolation and persistence is
destroyed by it. The intermediate regime — dense enough that a line is not one cut from extinction, not yet a single body that is cut together — is the best of
the three for a large lineage (0.298 against 0.250 and 0.137) and pays for it in generations (1.416 against 2.213). For the question this line exists to answer,
that is the first measured trade-off rather than a list of correlates: **the conditions of life here are not "more food" and not "more structure" but a place
between the two.**

Two things to keep: heredity is lowest exactly where persistence is highest (0.421 against 0.548 / 0.480, a note with ten worlds, and a control for X127), and
the plan's "non-monotone" label was measuring distance from the chord rather than monotonicity — the script now carries a position tag (`between` / `above` /
`below`) and the words are `bend` and `reversal`, the fourth vocabulary defect of the night and the same species as the other three.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x126-third-cell.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x126_third_cell.json`. Next: X127 counts cuts per lineage — the mechanism test for the
maze's low persistence on logs already on disk — and X128 places the depth threshold between 0.035 and 0.040.

## 10gu. A lineage usually dies by losing its name, not its cells — and the maze kills names more often (X127, 03.11)

X126 left a trade-off without a mechanism. The plan said "count cuts"; reading the tracker first said the useful distinction is narrower: a uid ends either by
**dividing** or by **disappearing**, and disappearing means either that the cells **vanish** or that they became **part of a neighbour** — absorption. The logged
areas decide which, with no new runs: absorbed if the surviving uids grow by ≥ 50 % of the dead uid's area at its death step, vanished if by ≤ 10 %, unclear
between. Thirty worlds, three cells, 2 470 identity deaths, all controlled (end-survival reproducing X126 to four decimals, heredity, an area budget that closes on
all 18 000 step transitions, and full coverage).

| cell | identity deaths | absorbed | vanished | unclear | median dead area | smaller-sister absorbed | larger-sister absorbed |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 524 | **84.5 %** | 8.4 % | 7.1 % | 27.0 cells | 248 pairs | 183 pairs |
| 0.035 | 844 | **78.7 %** | 12.4 % | 8.9 % | 55.5 cells | 167 | 168 |
| 0.040 | 1 102 | **75.7 %** | 16.5 % | 7.8 % | 114.0 cells | 187 | 98 |

**Neither of the two registered outcomes fired, and that is the result.** The plan asked "is the maze lethal or homogenising"; absorption falls rather than rises
(−8.9 points, under the declared 0.10) and vanishing rises from 8.4 % to 16.5 % without coming close to a majority. What the cells differ in is not the kind of
death but its **amount**: identity deaths ×2.1 and real vanishings ×4.1 (44 → 182) from the lattice to the maze, with the dead getting larger (median 27 → 114
cells). In all three habitats — including the percolating one, where one body fills 935 cells — **three of every four lineage deaths are absorptions**, i.e. the
cells live on and only the name ends. Every persistence number this line has published (X115, X116, X126) is therefore a measure of name-keeping, not of cell
survival, and the habitats differ in how often a name is lost.

The smaller sister's lineage ends by absorption more often than the larger's in both cells where the effect excludes zero (248:183 and 187:98; paired −0.0569 and
−0.0745, CI −0.101…−0.047). Read with X92/X93 — in a percolating body the large structure is the one about to be cut for real — the picture is consistent: the
small one is swallowed, the large one is destroyed.

The manuscript sentence is therefore neither "lethal" nor "homogenising": **across these habitats a lineage's death is usually the death of its name, not of its
cells — and the maze is where names are lost most often and most expensively.** Two rules came out of the run: a refusal rule must contain a **"neither" branch**
(my first verdict printed the second branch's label when only its first clause held), and a fate classification is published with its **unclear share** next to it.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x127-absorption.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x127_absorption.json`. Next: X128 measures the identity half-life (how long a name outlives
its own uids), X129 asks whether absorption is a route to takeover, and X130 locates the 182 real vanishings at the body's edge or in its interior.

## 10gv. The budget must be local, and with a local budget the maze is the destructive habitat (X128, X128b, 03.11)

§10gu classified a disappearance with the **world's** area budget: absorbed if total survivor area grew by at least half the dead structure's area. In a world of
twenty isolated spots that test is satisfied by growth somewhere else, so the lattice's "84.5 % absorbed" measured the world's book-keeping, not a transfer into a
neighbour. X128 caught it by accident — its own control demanded the absorbed share reproduce X127, and its partner census found no partner at all in 73 % of
lattice absorptions — and X128b rebuilt the instrument on the dead structure's own neighbourhood: survivors within `r_dead + r_survivor + 2` cells, plus the
category the lattice needed, a death with **no live neighbour at all**.

| cell | deaths | isolated | absorbed | vanished | unclear | kin of the absorber | unrelated |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 524 | **31.3 %** | 55.3 % (51.2–59.5) | **10.5 %** | 2.9 % | **87.2 %** of 290 | 12.8 % |
| 0.035 | 844 | 29.4 % | 47.8 % (44.6–51.3) | 21.7 % | 1.2 % | 39.2 % of 403 | 60.8 % |
| 0.040 | 1 102 | 21.2 % | 48.4 % (45.6–51.2) | **27.4 %** | 3.0 % | **37.7 %** of 531 | 62.3 % |

Controls: end-survival reproduces X126 to four decimals; heredity 0.548 / 0.421 / 0.480; the local and global accounts agree on **all 18 000 step transitions**;
the fates sum to the deaths; and the pre-declared disagreement with X127 fired (the lattice's absorbed share fell 29.2 points, against the required 20). The unclear
band collapsed from 7–9 % to 1–3 %, which is the measure of how much the world-level version was hiding in its residual.

**Correction to §10gu.** Absorption is about **half** of all endings everywhere (48–55 %), not three quarters, and the maze is **not** the milder habitat: real
vanishings rise 10.5 % → 21.7 % → 27.4 %, i.e. **55 → 183 → 302 events**, a factor of 5.5, while the lattice's deaths are three times more often **isolated**
(31.3 % against 21.2 %). The sentence that survives is narrower and better: *everywhere about half of a lineage's endings are absorptions; what changes is the
other half — the lattice's structures die alone, the maze's are destroyed.*

**And the kinship answer is decisive.** Where the cells are taken in the lattice, **87.2 %** go to kin and more than half to **siblings**: a split's daughters settle
next to each other and re-merge, so losing a name there is a family book-keeping event. In both percolating cells, **61–62 %** of absorptions go to **strangers**.
So the percolating habitat does two things to a lineage at once: it destroys more of them outright, and the ones it spares are **handed to non-relatives** — nothing
of what the lineage was can continue in the thing that took its cells.

Read with §10gt's trade-off, the three habitats now have characters rather than numbers: the lattice is a **family world** (isolated or sibling-absorbed deaths, the
slowest generations), the maze is a **market of strangers** (a quarter of endings destroy the cells, the rest change hands outside the family, the deepest lineages
and the worst persistence), and the middle cell has some of both and the best survival of a large lineage. For the question this line exists to answer that is the
first condition stated in terms of structure rather than amount: **persistence needs structures close enough to shield each other and separate enough that what
takes their cells is their own kin.**

Reports: `reports/2026-11-03-x128-kinship-void.md`, `-x128b-local.md`; aggregates `l1_field_x128_kinship.json`, `l1_field_x128b_local.json`. Next: X129 tests
whether the kin advantage is geometric (a world whose daughters are born further apart), X130 locates the maze's 302 vanishings at the edge or in the interior, X131
asks whether the absorber inherits the swallowed lineage's size.

## 10gw. Who lives next door: absorption always happens at contact, and the neighbour's genealogy is what the habitat changes (X129, 03.11)

§10gv left the sharpest number of the night unexplained: the absorber is kin in 87.2 % of lattice absorptions and a stranger in 61-62 % of the percolating ones. The
deflationary reading is geometry — daughters are born adjacent, so in a world of isolated spots the only structure within reach of a dying one is its own sibling.
Matching on contact distance was the planned test, and it degenerated: **every partnered absorption is a contact event** (100 % inside `delta <= 1.5`, median
`delta` 0.66-0.72, i.e. the bounding circles overlap), partly because the neighbourhood was itself defined as contact plus two cells. A match on a variable that the
instrument fixes is a tautology, and the rule is written down.

The degeneracy is more informative than the match would have been. Across all three cells the geometry of absorption is the same: the same contact distance, **one
neighbour within reach** (median neighbourhood size 1.0), and absorbers of the same order of size whether kin or stranger (65 against 83 cells in the lattice, 378
against 473 in the maze). What differs is the **genealogy of that one neighbour**: a sibling 87.2 % of the time in the lattice, a stranger 62 % of the time in the
body, a difference of 49.6 points at identical geometry.

> **The lattice segregates lineages in space; the percolating body mixes them. What breaks a lineage's continuity in the maze is not distance and not destruction
> alone — it is spatial mixing.**

That is the mechanism under §10gt's trade-off: the maze buys generations (+2.213) by keeping the body cuttable, and pays in persistence (+0.1372) because cuttability
means mixing, and mixing means a lineage's cells are taken by someone else. It is also the most concrete condition the line has produced for a habitat where a
lineage can persist: **a structure's neighbour must be its kin**, which requires the habitat to hold families apart, and percolation is precisely the mechanism that
stops it doing so.

Three tests were run against the kinship result and it survived all three: it is not a distance effect, not a size effect, and not a count-of-neighbours effect. The
middle cell sits between the endpoints (39.2 % kin) exactly as its survival (+0.2984, the highest of the three) requires.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x129-geometry.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x129_geometry.json`. Next: X130 locates the maze's 302 vanishings at the edge or in the interior,
X131 asks whether the absorber inherits the swallowed lineage's size, and X132 turns "spatial mixing" into a gradient — the share of a structure's neighbours that
are kin as a function of inflow.

## 10gx. Sisters rarely merge back, and the ones that do are over in 60-120 steps (X130, 03.11)

§10gv left a dynamic question: the lattice's absorber is a **sibling** in 64 % of partnered absorptions, but does that mean families continuously collapse into each
other? X130 started a clock at every split with two measurable daughters and asked whether, and when, one daughter's uid ends into the other's. Controls: the
sibling-absorption census reproduces §10gv **exactly** (186 / 31 / 57, computed independently by the second script), end-survival reproduces X126 to four decimals.

| cell | sister pairs | re-merged | share (95 % CI) | censored | median birth separation | merged / rest | median time (CI) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.030 | 1 142 | 210 | **18.4 %** (16.1-20.8) | 81.6 % | 8.99 cells | 8.06 / 9.27 | **80 steps** (70-90) |
| 0.035 | 1 106 | 95 | **8.6 %** (7.1-10.3) | 91.4 % | 12.62 cells | 9.46 / 13.35 | 120 steps (90-150) |
| 0.040 | 1 195 | 208 | **17.4 %** (15.2-19.5) | 82.6 % | 22.30 cells | **11.75 / 24.52** | **60 steps** (50-70) |

**Most sister pairs never meet again** inside the run (82-91 % censored), and **the merge is fast when it happens** — a median of 6-12 logging quanta. The two
headline numbers therefore do not contradict each other, they carry different denominators and both must be quoted with theirs: **among sister pairs** 18.4 % ever
re-merge, **among absorptions with a partner** 64 % take a sibling. The reconciliation is that absorption is a rare event concentrated where the daughters were born
close: in the maze the median birth separation is 22.3 cells, merged pairs were born at 11.75 and unmerged at 24.52 — a factor of two at the same inflow.

So the lattice is not a world of continuously collapsing families. It is a world where the **rare** collision that does happen is a family one, and it is over within
about eighty steps. Birth separation is the variable that decides it, which gives a future manipulation a handle.

The middle cell is again the outlier in the direction its survival requires: the **lowest** re-merge share (8.6 %) and the **fewest** sibling absorptions (31) in the
cell where a large lineage survives best (+0.2984). Fewer family collisions, more persistence — the third independent statistic to point that way.

Two defects in the run's own script, both caught by its controls and both worth a rule. The survival readout was first computed on the daughters in **birth order**
instead of the larger/smaller ordering, so the within-pair differences cancelled and it printed 0.0219 where X126 says 0.2496: when a statistic is a difference inside
a pair, the ordering must be applied in **every** block that uses the pair. And the interval printed next to the median time was a bootstrap of the **mean** — 80 with
[127, 211], an interval that does not contain its own point estimate: an interval belongs to the statistic it is printed with, and a bootstrap of a cousin is a
different claim, not a conservative version of this one.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x130-remerge.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x130_remerge.json`. Next: X131 asks whether the absorber inherits the swallowed lineage's size, and
X132 is the first interventional test of the whole chain — a world whose split distance is set larger, which should remove the family absorptions and, if §10gw is right,
raise persistence.

## 10gy. Ten worlds separate the death fates and the kinship, and do not separate the survival contrast (X131, 03.11)

Every interval in §10gt-§10gx was bootstrapped over pairs or deaths inside ten worlds. X131 recomputed each statistic **per world** and tested the cell differences
at the world level, with the three cells paired by seed (301-310). Controls: the pooled value recomputed from the per-world numerators and denominators reproduces
every published number exactly.

| statistic | lattice - maze | middle - lattice | maze - middle |
|---|---|---|---|
| vanished share | **+0.178** (0.128-0.224) | **+0.125** (0.082-0.178) | **+0.053** (0.012-0.095) |
| kin share | **-0.443** (-0.525 to -0.345) | **-0.427** (-0.527 to -0.311) | -0.016 (ns) |
| absorbed share | **-0.132** | **-0.136** | +0.004 (ns) |
| re-merge share | +0.022 (ns) | **-0.068** | **+0.090** |
| isolated share | -0.067 (ns) | +0.010 (ns) | **-0.077** |
| depth contrast | **+1.049** (0.114-2.112) | +0.091 (ns) | +0.958 (ns) |
| survival contrast | **-0.077** (-0.166 to **+0.012**, ns) | +0.074 (ns) | **-0.151** |

**World-level and quantitative:** the percolating cells destroy more (vanished rises monotonically, all three pairs), they hand their absorptions to strangers
(-0.443, a step), the lattice absorbs more (-0.13 against both), and the middle cell's low collision rate is real (lower than both neighbours).

**Marginal:** the depth contrast lattice-versus-maze survives at +1.049 with a lower bound of 0.114, and it does not resolve the middle cell from either neighbour -
exactly the step at percolation that §10gt reported.

**Not resolved with ten worlds, and this is a correction to the block's rhetoric.** The survival contrast between the lattice and the maze - +0.2496 against
+0.1372, the number §10gm, §10gn and §10gt all build on - is -0.077 across ten seed pairs with an interval that **crosses zero**. At the pair level it is 17 sigma;
at the world level the world-to-world spread (sd 0.14) is as large as the effect. What *is* resolved in survival is the fall from the middle cell into the maze
(-0.151). So the consequence claim has to be stated in two parts: **within a world** the larger sister's lineage survives more often (+0.25 in the lattice, +0.14 in
the maze), and **between habitats** only the middle-to-maze drop is established.

The practical consequence is a power requirement, not a new design: on the observed spread (sd 0.14 for survival, 0.69-1.67 for depth) a paired test at 80 % power
needs **30-40 worlds** per cell. That is the price of the next cross-habitat claim, and it is cheaper than a new world.

Two rules came out of the run. **Cells that share seeds must be compared paired** - overlapping marginal intervals is a weaker test of the same hypothesis and must
not be reported as *the* test, though both results should be printed. And **a pair-level interval is within-world precision**: quoting it as a habitat-level
statement is the same error as quoting a filtered statistic without its filter.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x131-worlds.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x131_worlds.json`. Next: X132 is the power run (thirty worlds per cell, the same flags, new
seeds), X133 pools the ten worlds with a world random effect to see how much of the within-world effect survives the world-level variance, and X134 asks what makes
the survival contrast swing between 0.09 and 0.30 across seeds.

## 10gz. Thirty worlds: the survival contrast is real, and larger than ten worlds said (X132, 03.11)

§10gy left the block's central contrast unresolved: with ten worlds the difference between the lattice and the maze in end-survival was -0.077 with an interval
crossing zero, and the power calculation put the requirement at `n >= 26`. X132 ran twenty new worlds per cell (seeds 311-330, added to the existing ten), anchor
exact key-by-key, all forty runs alive, every control held.

| statistic | lattice | maze | maze - lattice (paired, 30 seeds) | outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| end-survival contrast | 0.2960 (sd 0.1023) | 0.1636 (sd 0.1010) | **-0.1323** (-0.1819 to -0.0840) | **resolved** |
| depth contrast | 1.4889 (sd 0.5885) | 3.3229 (sd 2.2803) | **+1.8340** (1.0442 to 2.7231) | **resolved** |
| absorbed share | 0.5386 | 0.4430 | -0.0911 | resolved |
| isolated share | 0.2708 | 0.1930 | -0.0754 | resolved |
| vanished share | 0.0929 | 0.2347 | **+0.1419** | resolved |
| kin share | 0.8058 | 0.3894 | **-0.4164** | resolved |

**The ten-world estimate was biased low, not merely imprecise.** The paired difference grows from -0.077 to **-0.132** when the twenty new seeds are added, and the
per-world mean survival rises in both cells (lattice 0.222 to 0.296, maze 0.146 to 0.164). A ten-world estimate of a cross-habitat quantity is therefore not a
noisy version of the thirty-world one - it can sit a factor of 1.7 away from it - and the correction came from adding worlds, not from re-reading them.

**What this restores and what it costs.** §10gy's method stands and its conclusion is superseded: the survival contrast **is** a property of the habitat, at -0.132
with the interval comfortably away from zero, and the depth contrast is larger than the ten-world estimate (+1.83 against +1.05) with a per-world spread in the maze
(sd 2.28) four times the lattice's (0.59) - the maze is deeper **and** far more variable. The whole identity block is confirmed at the world level in the same run:
the lattice absorbs more (0.539 against 0.443), dies alone more often (0.271 against 0.193), loses fewer cells outright (0.093 against 0.235) and keeps its
absorptions in the family (0.806 against 0.389).

The run cost forty worlds and fifty minutes, which makes the rule affordable: **any cross-habitat claim carries a power calculation before it is written**, and ten
worlds is the default only for within-world effects whose inference already runs over thousands of pairs.

Report: `reports/2026-11-03-x132-power.md`; aggregate `l1_field_x132_power.json`. Next: X133 asks why the maze's depth is heavy-tailed (sd 2.28 against a mean of
3.32), and X134 is the first interventional test of the chain - two structures seeded at a controlled separation, swept, to find the distance at which the habitat
stops keeping them apart.

## 10ha. The mixing length is one pattern wavelength, and it is a lever rather than a correlate (X134, 03.11)

Every result in §10gv-§10gx is observational: the separation between lineages and the kinship of a neighbour are whatever the world produces. X134 is the block's first
**intervention**. A new additive initial condition (`--init-spots R:SEP`, anchored bit-for-bit when absent) places two discs and nothing else - radius 3 cells, 29 cells
